# Abstract

Over every algebraically closed trivially valued field of characteristic zero, let $X$ be a smooth integral projective variety and let $L$ be any big rational line bundle. Fix, before a target measure is chosen, an actual decomposition $L=A+D$, where $A$ is an ample rational line bundle and $D$ is an effective rational Cartier divisor on $X$. We reconstruct the entire genuine cone dominated by the canonical section reference $\sigma_D$: it is exactly $\sigma_D+\operatorname{PSH}(X,A)$, with a unique actual ample quotient, including its values on the reference divisor. The reconstruction uses one defining FS net, rational rounding, a finite polytope calculation, elementary convexity on scaling rays, literal section division, and compactness exclusively in a fixed actual ample cone. It does not use the false general extended-valued flattening lemma in BJ v4.

The actual local metric isometry off $D$, followed by reference floors, constructs its genuine nonpluripolar mixed intersection operator on the original $X$. The raw mass is $(A^n)$, explicitly tied to the prescribed reference. Relative energy, the intrinsic target class, two-sided outer derivatives in every continuous original direction, comparison, and an attained equation with uniqueness modulo constants follow with their precise original-carrier interpretations. The separately proved zero-volume range is the actual trivial bundle, whose genuine cone consists of constants, whose operator and target mass are zero, and whose energy is zero.

This is an unverified proof draft relative to the task's exact actual-ample theorem G0. No result is inferred from a historical verdict, hash, compilation, or finite test. The theorem covers every big rational $L$ with each fixed datum above. It does not construct the minimal-singularity equation of mass $\operatorname{vol}(L)$, arbitrary reference types, or the equation theory for every nonbig pseudoeffective class. These residual interpretations of original P16.5 remain explicit in the final scope assessment.

# 1. Exact domain, reference and statement

Let $k$ be algebraically closed, trivially valued, and of characteristic zero, with no restriction on cardinality. Let $X$ be smooth, integral, projective over $k$, of dimension $n\geq1$. All analytic functions below are actual functions on the compact Hausdorff Berkovich space $X^{\rm an}$. Rational bundles mean elements of $\operatorname{Pic}(X)\otimes\mathbb Q$, cleared by positive integral tensor powers whenever sections occur. Potentials are measured from the canonical trivial metric on the indicated actual bundle. All measures are positive Radon measures unless called signed. Intersections use $[X]$ and have no probability factor.

For an actual rational bundle $B$, a generically finite real FS function is
$$f=m^{-1}\max_j\{\log\|s_j\|_{\rm triv}+\lambda_j\},\qquad
s_j\in H^0(X,mB)\setminus\{0\},\quad\lambda_j\in\mathbb R.\tag{1.1}$$
The displayed family need not generate $mB$. It generates exactly when $f$ is finite everywhere. Nonzero sections suffice for generic finiteness on integral $X$. The canonical norms lie in $[0,1]$, and section logarithms take the value $-\infty$ on their zero schemes. The trivial valuation of $k(X)$ is denoted $v_0$.

For $\theta\in N^1(X)$ we use the literal defining cone: $\phi\in\operatorname{PSH}_{\rm def}(\theta)$ means that $\phi$ is generically finite and is the all-point limit of one decreasing net of functions (1.1) on actual rational $B_i$ such that $c_1(B_i)\to\theta$. This is BJ Definition 4.1, not an invocation of its subsequent varying-class closure theorem. Its defining limits are usc, since extended-continuous FS functions are usc. Write $\operatorname{PSH}(L)$ for this cone with $\theta=c_1(L)$. Within an actual ample bundle this is the genuine ample cone in G0. We do not assume that arbitrary nested varying-class limits flatten.

Choose and retain actual data
$$L\simeq A+\mathcal O_X(D),\quad A\text{ ample rational},\quad D\geq0\text{ rational Cartier}.\tag{1.2}$$
Here and below additive bundle notation includes the specified rational isomorphism. Section 2 constructs such data for every big rational $L$ on this same $X$, without Bertini or modifications. Define
$$\sigma=\sigma_D=\log\|s_D\|_{\rm triv},\quad
Z=\{\sigma=-\infty\}=|D|^{\rm an},\quad U=X^{\rm an}\setminus Z,\quad V_D=(A^n)>0.\tag{1.3}$$
If $D=0$, set $\sigma=0$ and $Z=\varnothing$. The rational logarithm is defined by any cleared multiple and is independent of that multiple. Scaling acts by $\sigma(tv)=t\sigma(v)$, including at $-\infty$. The reference and all operators are fixed before selecting a target. Define the entire prescribed-reference cone
$$\mathcal P_D(L)=\{u\in\operatorname{PSH}(L):u\leq\sigma+C
\text{ on all }X^{\rm an}\text{ for some }C\in\mathbb R\}.\tag{1.4}$$

**Theorem 1 (all-big prescribed divisorial-reference equation).** For every datum just specified:

1. The map $F_D(\psi)=\sigma+\psi$ is an order-preserving bijection from the actual genuine $\operatorname{PSH}(X,A)$ onto (1.4). Its inverse $Q_D$ is the unique actual $A$-psh extension of $u-\sigma$ from $U$; values on $Z$ are uniquely determined, not arbitrarily assigned. The supremum of either potential is its value at $v_0$. The normalized spaces are homeomorphic in the divisorial topology, and are compact. Relative envelopes are genuine and continuous in their quotient for continuous quotient obstacles.
2. On the original $X$, the genuine local metric products on $U$, extended by reference-floor nonpluripolar limits, give symmetric positive raw mixed measures. For $u_i\in\mathcal E_D^1$ on the same datum their mass is $V_D$; for different fixed data $L_i=A_i+D_i$ their mixed mass is $(A_1\cdots A_n)$. These products equal the genuine ample mixed products of the unique quotients, because of an actual local metric isometry and removal of the pole sets. They have unbounded decreasing-net continuity and the stated integrable quotient-weighted continuity.
3. The actual relative energy is
$$E_D(u)=\frac1{n+1}\sum_{j=0}^n\int Q_Du\,T_u^j\wedge T_\sigma^{n-j}
 =E_{\rm raw,A}(Q_Du),\tag{1.5}$$
first for bounded quotients, then by reference floors. Its space $\mathcal E_D^1=\{u\in\mathcal P_D(L):E_D(u)>-\infty\}$ consists of actual singular metrics. One has $E_D(\sigma)=0$ and $E_D(u+c)=E_D(u)+V_Dc$.
4. The intrinsic target condition is
$$\mu(X^{\rm an})=V_D,\qquad
D_D(\mu):=\sup_{u\in\mathcal E_D^1}
\left(E_D(u)-\int Q_Du\,d\mu\right)<\infty.\tag{1.6}$$
It is equivalent to integrability of every actual quotient $Q_Du$ with $u\in\mathcal E_D^1$. It implies $\mu(Z)=0$; no support restriction is imposed separately. For every such target an actual $u\in\mathcal E_D^1$ satisfies $\operatorname{MA}_D(u)=\mu$, and all solutions differ by a real constant. The dual supremum is attained. Comparison and domination hold for this original cone and operator.
5. For every $u\in\mathcal E_D^1$ and every $f\in C^0(X^{\rm an})$ the reference outer energy satisfies
$$\left.\frac d{dt}\right|_{t=0}E_D^{\downarrow}(u+tf)
 =\int f\,\operatorname{MA}_D(u),\tag{1.7}$$
with both signs of $t$. Its definition retains the reference even where $u=-\infty$.

**Theorem 2 (actual trivial-bundle zero-volume range).** For $L=\mathcal O_X$, the literal genuine numerical zero cone is $\operatorname{PSH}(0)=\mathbb R$. With reference $0$, the raw energy is zero and the raw operator is the zero measure. Its intrinsic positive target class consists solely of $\mu=0$. Every constant solves that equation, and uniqueness modulo constants holds. There is no division by volume and no probability normalization. This assertion is not made for every zero-volume pseudoeffective bundle.

Theorem 1 includes every big nonample rational bundle, every fixed datum (1.2), and every target satisfying (1.6). It does not identify different choices of $D$ or their masses. It concerns a prescribed divisorial reference, rather than claiming that all of $\operatorname{PSH}(L)$ has been given a reference-free mass or energy. Section 11 distinguishes this complete theorem from the full original axis.

# 2. Actual no-Bertini data for every big rational bundle

Use the numerical conventions in the supplied BJ source: $\operatorname{Psef}(X)$ is the closed cone generated by effective real Cartier divisor classes, and $\operatorname{Big}(X)$ is its interior. The ample cone is open in finite-dimensional $N^1(X)$. Ampleness of an actual rational bundle is numerical on our projective $X$: a numerically ample actual representative is ample. This algebraic fact is an external premise with its precise primary scope recorded in Appendix A; it is not a nonample analytic grant.

Let $\theta=c_1(L)$ be any rational big class. Fix an actual ample integral $H$. For sufficiently small rational $\varepsilon>0$, $\theta-\varepsilon c_1(H)$ lies in $\operatorname{Psef}(X)$. Since $\varepsilon c_1(H)$ is ample and that cone is open, an effective rational Cartier divisor $D$ can be chosen with $c_1(D)$ close enough to $\theta-\varepsilon c_1(H)$ that
$$\theta-c_1(D)\in\operatorname{Amp}(X).\tag{2.1}$$
Here is why the approximation is literal. The defining closed effective cone is the closure of finite nonnegative real combinations of effective Cartier divisors. Each such finite combination can be approximated by nonnegative rational coefficients. On smooth $X$ the prime components are Cartier, so the approximation is an actual effective rational Cartier divisor. No infinite divisor, finitely generated section ring, or change of field is introduced. Set $A=L-\mathcal O_X(D)$ as an actual rational bundle. By numerical ampleness, (2.1) makes this actual $A$ ample. The identity (1.2) is then literal in $\operatorname{Pic}(X)_{\mathbb Q}$, not merely numerical.

This proves existence for every big actual rational bundle, even with arbitrary numerically trivial twists. It uses neither Bertini nor a birational model. It also avoids confusing top self-intersection with volume. Conversely, actual $A+D$ data are big: the ample cone lies in the interior of the effective cone (sufficiently high ample powers have sections, and small rational perturbations remain ample), and adding an effective class preserves that interior. The data therefore have exactly the required big domain.

We will repeatedly use the elementary section fact that for a generating finite family $t_j$ of a cleared ample power,
$$\max_j\log\|t_j\|_{\rm triv}=0\quad\text{at every analytic point}.\tag{2.2}$$
At the valuation-ring center, every section is a regular local coefficient of a frame of norm one, hence has norm at most one. A generating section is a unit at that center and has norm one. This includes points with nongeneric support. Global generation of sufficiently large ample powers has precisely the hypotheses of Stacks 01Q3; those hypotheses hold on every projective $X$ here. Products, powers and union of the finitely many displayed section families respect their canonical norms and actual bundles.

# 3. Direct foundations from a single defining FS net

## 3.1. Rational rounding without nested closure

Let $\phi_i\downarrow\phi$ be its one defining real FS net in $B_i$. For a positive rational $\delta$, round every finite coefficient $\lambda_{ij}$ upward to a rational number with error at most $m_i\delta$. The corresponding actual rational FS function $f_{i,\delta}$ satisfies
$$\phi_i\leq f_{i,\delta}\leq\phi_i+\delta.\tag{3.1}$$
These inequalities are non-strict and are valid also at common section zeros. Put $g_{i,\delta}=f_{i,\delta}+\delta$. Order pairs by increasing $i$, decreasing $\delta$, and decreasing $g$ pointwise. This set is directed: given two pairs, take $i_3$ above both and $2\delta_3\leq\min(\delta_1,\delta_2)$. Then
$$g_{i_3,\delta_3}\leq\phi_{i_3}+2\delta_3
\leq\min(\phi_{i_1}+\delta_1,\phi_{i_2}+\delta_2)
\leq\min(g_{i_1,\delta_1},g_{i_2,\delta_2}).\tag{3.2}$$
The new net decreases to $\phi$ everywhere: at a finite value choose an original index and then a sufficiently small $\delta$; at $-\infty$ use any finite threshold. The index map is increasing and final onto the original index set, and $\delta\to0$, so its actual bundle classes still tend to $\theta$. If $\phi(v_0)\geq0$, the new approximants retain nonnegative generic values. This repairs rational approximation directly, without BJ Lemma 4.6.

## 3.2. Embedding every defining numerical function into an actual ample cone

Choose an actual rational ample $H$ with $c_1(H)-\theta$ ample. Eventually $H-B_i$ is an actual ample rational bundle. After clearing degrees, multiply every displayed $B_i$-section by a generating family of a sufficiently divisible power of $H-B_i$. Formula (2.2) makes the new actual $H$-section maximum exactly $\phi_i$, including all its poles. Thus the decreasing tail is a defining FS net on the single actual ample $H$, and
$$\phi\in\operatorname{PSH}(X,H).\tag{3.3}$$
Finite lists of numerical functions can be placed in one common $H$. The ample grant now gives finiteness on every divisorial point, $\sup\phi=\phi(v_0)$, all-point divisorial determination, and the implication from a divisorial inequality to an arbitrary usc obstacle. These consequences are valid for our defining numerical functions by (3.3); no nonample closure theorem is being assumed.

## 3.3. Homogeneous projection by a finite polytope

For $\phi(v_0)\geq0$, set
$$\widehat\phi(v)=\inf_{t>0}\frac{\phi(tv)}t.\tag{3.4}$$
This operation preserves order, is homogeneous, and commutes with decreasing infima: $\inf_i\inf_t=\inf_t\inf_i$, also in the extended real sense. For a rational FS function (1.1), put $x_j=\log\|s_j\|(v)$, and write $r=1/t$. Then
$$m\widehat\phi(v)=\inf_{r\geq0}\max_j(x_j+r\lambda_j)
=\max_{w\in W}\sum_jw_jx_j,\tag{3.5}$$
where $W$ is the finite vertex set of
$$P=\{w_j\geq0,\ \sum_jw_j=1,\ \sum_jw_j\lambda_j\geq0\}.\tag{3.6}$$
The infimum for $r>0$ equals that for $r\geq0$ by continuity at zero. For finite $x_j$, every feasible weighted expression is at most the maximum for every $r$. Conversely a minimum of the convex piecewise affine function in $r$ occurs at zero, at a finite breakpoint, or asymptotically at infinity. At zero an active nonnegative slope gives an eligible unit vertex. At a breakpoint a zero active slope or two opposite active slopes with their zero weighted average gives a vertex attaining the same value. The asymptotic finite case has largest slope zero and is attained by an active zero-slope unit vertex. These cases prove equality. The vertices are the eligible unit vectors and the zero averages of two opposite slopes; their coefficients are rational.

Choose a common denominator $b$ of the weights. The monomials
$$S_w=\prod_j s_j^{bw_j}\in H^0(X,mbB),\qquad w\in W,\tag{3.7}$$
are actual nonzero sections on integral $X$. Their homogeneous FS maximum, divided by $mb$, is (3.5). Zero exponents mean omission of the section, so no $0\cdot(-\infty)$ ambiguity occurs. If some $x_j=-\infty$, replace them by $\max(x_j,-N)$ in (3.5), commute the infima over $N$ and $r$, and then pass the fixed finite maximum of weighted sums to its decreasing limit. This proves the same formula at every base-locus point, including the case where it equals $-\infty$.

Apply this calculation to the rational net of Section 3.1. The homogeneous actual FS functions decrease to $\widehat\phi$, have generic value zero, and have actual classes tending to $\theta$. They therefore prove $\widehat\phi\in\operatorname{PSH}_{\rm def}(\theta)$ by the original single-net definition. No varying-class closure or printed homogeneous approximation theorem is needed. If $h$ is homogeneous, $\widehat h=h$.

## 3.4. All-point homogeneous decomposition

Let $C=\phi(v_0)$ and, for $\lambda<C$, put $h_\lambda=\widehat{\phi-\lambda}$. It is a genuine homogeneous numerical $\theta$-psh function with generic value zero, and $h_\lambda+\lambda\leq\phi$. At every actual point $v$,
$$\phi(v)=\sup_{\lambda<C}\{h_\lambda(v)+\lambda\}.\tag{3.8}$$
To prove the reverse inequality, consider $g(t)=\phi(tv)$ on $(0,\infty)$. Each defining FS function on this ray is convex, decreasing, and bounded above by its generic value. The decreasing limit has the same properties. If it is $-\infty$ at one point, convexity using a larger point with a finite uniform upper bound forces $-\infty$ at every smaller point, and monotonicity forces it at every larger point. Thus the ray is either identically $-\infty$ or finite convex. The first case proves (3.8) immediately.

In the finite case choose a supporting line $at+\lambda_0$ at $t=1$. Its slope is nonpositive and its intercept is at most $C$, by taking $t\downarrow0$ in the inequality $at+\lambda_0\leq g(t)\leq C$. If $\lambda_0<C$, (3.4) gives $h_{\lambda_0}(v)\geq a$, so equality at $t=1$ proves (3.8). If $\lambda_0=C$, lower its intercept to $C-\epsilon$: the same line inequality gives $h_{C-\epsilon}(v)\geq a$, and the right side of (3.8) is at least $g(1)-\epsilon$. Let $\epsilon\downarrow0$. This proves (3.8) on all support types and pole points without BJ 6.24.

# 4. The entire dominated cone and its unique actual quotient

## 4.1. Forward membership, including common poles

G0 supplies a decreasing net of finite continuous actual $A$-FS functions $\psi_j$ for every $\psi\in\operatorname{PSH}(X,A)$. Clear the degree of $\psi_j$ and the denominators of $A,D,L$ to one integer $p$. Multiply the displayed $pA$-sections by the canonical section of $pD$. Their actual $pL$-section maximum is exactly
$$\sigma+\psi_j.\tag{4.1}$$
The net decreases on every analytic point to $\sigma+\psi$. On $Z$ all these maxima are exactly $-\infty$. It proves genuine membership by the single defining $L$-net, and $\sigma+\psi\leq\sigma+\sup\psi$. Thus $F_D$ maps into the full cone (1.4).

For completeness, finite fixed-bundle flattening, when needed solely in this ample cone, is legitimate. Suppose $\psi_i\downarrow\psi$, and each $\psi_i$ is a decreasing limit of finite continuous $A$-FS functions $f_{ij}$. Order triples $(i,j,\epsilon)$ by increasing $i$ and decreasing $f_{ij}+\epsilon$. For two triples take a larger outer index and a strictly smaller positive $\epsilon$. The limiting lower function plus that $\epsilon$ is strictly below the minimum of the two finite upper functions everywhere, even where the limit is $-\infty$. The continuous exponential differences decrease to a strictly negative function. Their negative open sets cover compact $X^{\rm an}$; a finite subcover and inner directedness give one lower finite approximant globally. This makes the triples directed and their FS functions decrease to $\psi$ (use arbitrary finite thresholds at $-\infty$). This restricted argument has finite upper inner functions. We never apply its strict step to (4.1) at their common poles. G0 already supplies the required finite fixed-$A$ approximation; this argument explains why that use does not import the failed general lemma.

## 4.2. Homogeneous division using compactness, not divided-net monotonicity

Let $h$ be homogeneous, genuine $L$-psh, and $h\leq\sigma$. If $D=0$, $L=A$ and the assertion is the ample identification. Assume $D\ne0$. Section 3.3 gives decreasing homogeneous actual rational FS $h_i$ in $B_i$ with $c_1(B_i)\to c_1(L)$ and $h_i(v_0)=0$. The set
$$K=\{\sigma=-1\}\tag{4.2}$$
is compact: $\sigma$ is continuous into $\mathbb R\cup\{-\infty\}$ and $\{-1\}$ is closed. It is nonempty, since a component valuation of $D$ can be scaled to it. For every rational $t\in(0,1)$, the open sets $\{h_i<-t\}\cap K$ increase and cover $K$, since $h\leq-1$ there. Compactness yields an entire tail with $h_i<-t$ on $K$.

Use the directed cofinal pairs $(i,t)$ satisfying this condition, ordered by increasing $i$ and $t$, with $t\to1$. Homogeneity gives $h_i(v)\leq t\sigma(v)$ on each divisorial $v$: scale $v$ to $K$ if $\sigma(v)<0$, and use $h_i\leq0$ if $\sigma(v)=0$. A fixed actual ample embedding for each $h_i$ and its usc obstacle gives
$$h_i\leq t\sigma\quad\text{on all }X^{\rm an}.\tag{4.3}$$
This application needs no ample membership of $t\sigma$: it is an usc obstacle.

Apply literal section division to (4.3). After clearing degrees, each displayed section of $pB_i$ vanishes along every prime component of $ptD$ to at least its specified multiplicity, as seen by its prime divisor valuation. Normality of smooth $X$ implies that division by $s_{ptD}$ gives an actual global section of $p(B_i-tD)$. Equivalently, in a normal trivializing affine ring a rational coefficient with no pole at any height-one prime is regular; this is exactly the normality input in BJ Lemma 2.23 and its preserved proof. Therefore
$$r_{i,t}=h_i-t\sigma\quad\text{on }U\tag{4.4}$$
has a literal homogeneous FS extension on $B_i-tD$, given by these quotient sections, and generic value zero. Formula (4.4) is not an extended subtraction on $Z$.

The divided functions need not decrease. Instead adjoin rational $\eta>0$ and retain triples with
$$c=1+\eta,\qquad cA-(B_i-tD)\text{ actual ample}.\tag{4.5}$$
Order them by increasing $i,t$ and decreasing $\eta$. They are directed and cofinal with $t\to1,\eta\to0$: for any smaller positive $\eta$, take $t$ close enough to one and $i$ sufficiently late that $B_i-tD$ is numerically close enough to $A$ for (4.5), using openness of the ample cone. Increasing $i$ again retains the compact $K$ condition. After rational scaling and multiplication by a generating family of a cleared power of the ample difference (4.5), (2.2) realizes
$$a_{i,t,\eta}=c^{-1}r_{i,t}\in\operatorname{PSH}(X,A),\qquad a_{i,t,\eta}(v_0)=0.\tag{4.6}$$
These are actual normalized $A$-FS functions.

G0 compactness gives a subnet converging to an actual $r\in\operatorname{PSH}(X,A)$ in the divisorial topology. At every divisorial point, the values in (4.6) tend explicitly to $h(v)-\sigma(v)$: $h_i(v)\to h(v)$, $t\to1$, $c\to1$, and both $h(v)$ and $\sigma(v)$ are finite there. Hence $r=h-\sigma$ on divisorial points. By Section 4.1, $\sigma+r$ is genuine $L$-psh. Put it and $h$ into one actual ample $H$ by Section 3.2. Ample divisorial determination gives
$$h=\sigma+r\quad\text{at every analytic point}.\tag{4.7}$$
In particular $h=-\infty$ on $Z$, as required. Divisorial determination in the actual ample $A$ proves uniqueness of $r$.

## 4.3. General dominated functions

Let $u\in\mathcal P_D(L)$, so $u\leq\sigma+C_0$, and put $C=u(v_0)$. Section 3.4 gives $u=\sup_{\lambda<C}(h_\lambda+\lambda)$ at all points. At a divisorial point, evaluate $h_\lambda+\lambda\leq u\leq\sigma+C_0$ on $tv$, divide by $t$, and let $t\to\infty$. This gives $h_\lambda\leq\sigma$ divisorially, then everywhere by the ample embedding and usc obstacle principle. Section 4.2 gives actual $r_\lambda\in\operatorname{PSH}(X,A)$ with
$$h_\lambda=\sigma+r_\lambda,\qquad r_\lambda(v_0)=0.\tag{4.8}$$
Thus $r_\lambda+\lambda\leq C$ everywhere.

We reconstruct its ample supremum without assuming a nonample supremum theorem. For finite nonempty $J\subset\{\lambda<C\}$, let
$$b_J=\max_{\lambda\in J}(r_\lambda+\lambda),\qquad c_J=\max J.\tag{4.9}$$
They are genuine actual $A$-psh, $b_J(v_0)=c_J$, and $c_J\uparrow C$. The normalized $b_J-c_J$ have a compact convergent subnet by G0. On each divisorial point, $b_J(v)$ increases to a finite value: it is bounded above by $C$ and below by any one fixed $r_\lambda(v)+\lambda$. The subnet therefore gives an actual $r\in\operatorname{PSH}(X,A)$ with
$$r(v)=\sup_{\lambda<C}(r_\lambda(v)+\lambda)\quad(v\in X^{\rm div}),\qquad r(v_0)=C.\tag{4.10}$$
For each $\lambda$, ample divisorial domination yields $r_\lambda+\lambda\leq r$ at every point. Taking the usc regularization of the supremum gives $\sup^*(r_\lambda+\lambda)\leq r$. The reverse inequality follows from (4.10) and the ample usc obstacle principle. Hence $r$ is exactly that usc supremum. On divisorial points (3.8) gives $u=\sigma+r$. Both sides are genuine $L$-psh and lie in a common ample $H$. Ample divisorial determination proves equality on all $X^{\rm an}$. This proves surjectivity of $F_D$ for the entire cone (1.4), not merely for a selected FS subcone.

If $F_D(\psi_1)=F_D(\psi_2)$, subtract the finite $\sigma$ on divisorial points and use ample determination to obtain $\psi_1=\psi_2$ everywhere. Likewise $F_D(\psi_1)\leq F_D(\psi_2)$ iff $\psi_1\leq\psi_2$. In the forward implication, the pole values cause no subtraction: divisorial inequalities suffice. This proves the claimed order bijection and the unique all-point quotient $Q_D$.

# 5. Normalization, compactness and genuine relative envelopes

The generic normalization is exact:
$$u(v_0)=Q_Du(v_0),\qquad\sup u=u(v_0),\qquad\sup Q_Du=Q_Du(v_0).\tag{5.1}$$
The equality of suprema for $u$ uses Section 3.2 and G0, rather than a heuristic density argument. On every divisorial coordinate $F_D$ is addition of the fixed finite number $\sigma(v)$, and $Q_D$ is subtraction of that number. Thus the maps are inverse homeomorphisms in the stated divisorial topology. G0 compactness of the normalized actual ample cone proves compactness of the original normalized cone (1.4). These are actual functions; there is no completion object or modification.

A family in (1.4) uniformly bounded above has uniformly bounded generic values, hence uniformly bounded quotients, by (5.1). Conversely uniformly bounded quotients imply a common bound by $\sigma+C$. Finite maxima and reference-relative convex combinations commute with $F_D$; at $Z$ all sums take value $-\infty$, and elsewhere this is ordinary arithmetic. If $u_i\downarrow u$ in this cone and the endpoint is generically finite, their quotients decrease: order reflection gives the order, G0 ample decreasing closure gives an actual limit, and ample determination identifies its image with $u$ on every point. This is a proved fixed-reference closure, not arbitrary varying-class closure.

For any usc $b$ bounded above, the exact envelope correspondence is
$$P_D(\sigma+b)=F_D(P_A(b)).\tag{5.2}$$
Indeed $F_D(\psi)\leq\sigma+b$ implies $\psi\leq b$ on divisorial points and then everywhere by the ample usc obstacle principle. Conversely $\psi\leq b$ implies the original inequality, including at $Z$. The envelopes and their usc regularizations consequently match; for a uniformly bounded family $\psi_\alpha$,
$$\sup^*_\alpha F_D(\psi_\alpha)=F_D(\sup^*_\alpha\psi_\alpha).\tag{5.3}$$
On $U$, $\sigma$ is finite continuous, so usc regularization commutes with its addition. At $Z$, the common upper bound on quotients and $\sigma\to-\infty$ force both sides to be $-\infty$. Thus G0 EP supplies genuine relative EP at every point. For continuous $b$ the quotient $P_A(b)$ is continuous, and (5.2) is a continuous singular metric in the specified reference sense. It is not a globally finite metric on a nonample $L$.

# 6. Genuine metric intersections and reference-floor extension

## 6.1. The actual local metric isometry

Potentials use $\|e\|_\psi=e^{-\psi}\|e\|_{\rm triv}$. Clear rational powers and write in an actual local frame $s_D=f e_D$, $e_L=e_Ae_D$. For $u=\sigma+\psi$, the local logarithmic metric weight is
$$h_L=-\log\|e_L\|_u
 =\psi-\log\|e_A\|_{\rm triv}+\log|f|
 =h_A+\log|f|.\tag{6.1}$$
On $U$, the regular nowhere vanishing section $s_D$ identifies $A|_U$ with $L|_U$. The frame $e_As_D=f e_L$ has weight $h_L-\log|f|=h_A$. Thus the two actual metrized bundles are locally isometric. The sign in (6.1) is plus. A claim with the opposite sign would describe the wrong metric.

The public CLD unit states that the log norm of an invertible analytic function is smooth with zero $d'd''$; its continuous Bedford--Taylor product is invariant under these harmonic frame terms. Consequently actual continuous quotient metrics have on $U$ exactly the genuine original $L$ curvature products of their $A$ metrics, not an inverse measure assigned after choosing a target. For actual $A$-FS metrics the regularized maxima of BE 7.12 give smooth semipositive approximants; BE 7.14 applies to semiample bundles over complete fields including the trivial valuation. BE 7.8, which assumes a nontrivial valuation, is not invoked. BE 8.3--8.5 give the genuine positive fundamental-cycle products and uniform continuity. G0 identifies these continuous products with its genuine intersection operator. All these hypotheses hold for the actual ample $A$.

For bounded, possibly discontinuous actual $\psi_i\in\operatorname{PSH}(A_i)$, take decreasing finite continuous FS nets supplied by G0. On $U=X^{\rm an}\setminus\bigcup Z_i$, transport those metrics by (6.1) first. The local products have the limit
$$T_{u_1}\wedge\cdots\wedge T_{u_n}|_U
 =\bigwedge_{i=1}^n(c_1(A_i)+dd^c\psi_i)|_U.\tag{6.2}$$
For a continuous test with compact support in $U$, extend it by zero to $X^{\rm an}$ and apply G0 mixed decreasing-net convergence. This proves independence of the chosen global approximants and actual local original-carrier agreement. It constructs the bounded metric intersection in this cone; it does not purport to prove a local Bedford--Taylor theorem for unrelated arbitrary local psh inputs.

## 6.2. A polar test, with the capacity exponent made explicit

We need to justify removal of $Z_i$ for unbounded finite-energy products and targets. The following proof uses only actual ample cones. For any actual ample $A$, choose $q$ and a nonzero $s\in H^0(X,qA)$ vanishing along $|D|$. Such a section exists by global generation of $\mathcal I_{|D|}\otimes qA$ for large $q$, and is nonzero at some point outside $|D|$. Set $a=q^{-1}\log\|s\|_{\rm triv}$, with $a(v_0)=0$. Its floors $\max(a,-j)$ are finite actual $A$-FS functions, obtained by adding a sufficiently divisible generating section family of constant weight $-j$. Their decreasing limit is genuine $A$-psh by G0. This construction imposes no extra condition on $D$.

Normalize ample Monge--Ampere measures to probability only inside this positive-mass estimate and write
$$\operatorname{Cap}_A(B)=\sup_{-1\leq b\leq0}\operatorname{MA}_{\rm BJ,A}(b)(B).$$
For every normalized ample $a$, bounded $-1\leq b\leq0$ satisfies $\int(-a)\operatorname{MA}_{\rm BJ}(b)\leq n$. At finite stages this follows by telescoping the difference from $\operatorname{MA}(0)=\delta_{v_0}$, integration by parts, and $-1\leq b\leq0$: each signed difference of two positive mixed terms of normalized mass one is bounded in absolute value by one. Floor approximation and monotone convergence give the assertion for unbounded $a$. This is the ample content of the preserved BJ 7.32 argument. Therefore
$$\operatorname{Cap}_A\{a\leq-t\}\leq n/t.\tag{6.3}$$

For a normalized ample potential $b$, let $b_s=\max(b,-s)$ and $\mu_s=\operatorname{MA}_{\rm BJ}(b_s)$. Energy comparison gives $J(b_s)\leq\int(-b_s)d\mu_s$. Locality and equal mass imply $\mu_s\{b\leq-t\}=\mu_t\{b\leq-t\}$ for $1\leq t\leq s$. Since $t^{-1}b_t$ is actual $A$-psh and lies in $[-1,0]$, expansion of its positive mixed product gives $\operatorname{MA}(t^{-1}b_t)\geq t^{-n}\mu_t$. Hence
$$J(b)\leq1+\int_1^\infty t^n\operatorname{Cap}_A\{b\leq-t\}\,dt.\tag{6.4}$$
The weight $t^n$ must be retained; some supplied renderer lines omit it. We use the strictly sufficient exponent $0<\alpha<1/(n+1)$, avoiding reliance on the more liberal printed exponent in a corollary.

The convex increasing function $\chi(x)=1-(1-x)^\alpha$, $x\leq0$, has $0\leq\chi'\leq1$. Its composition $b=\chi(a)$ is genuine $A$-psh: approximate $\chi$ decreasingly by convex piecewise affine chord majorants on expanding compact intervals, with constant left tails and slopes in $[0,1]$, use ample convexity, and then G0 fixed-ample decreasing closure. It is normalized and $b=-\infty$ on $Z$. By (6.3),
$$\operatorname{Cap}_A\{b\leq-t\}=O(t^{-1/\alpha}).$$
Since $n-1/\alpha<-1$, (6.4) gives $b\in\mathcal E^1(X,A)$. Thus $Z$ is contained in the pole set of an actual ample finite-energy potential. The same proof applies to any pole set defined by a numerical potential: Section 3.2 first embeds it into some actual ample $H$; scaling into $A$ can be done directly on its defining sections after adding generating sections of an ample difference. All steps occur inside fixed ample cones after that embedding.

G0 mixed integrability now proves that every finite-energy ample mixed measure gives zero mass to these polar sets: it integrates the just constructed finite-energy test. Likewise any target that integrates every actual ample $\mathcal E^1$ potential gives zero mass to $Z$. No unproved negligible-set theorem, Choquet extraction, or countability assumption is needed.

## 6.3. Reference floors and raw mass

For arbitrary $u=F_D(\psi)$ define genuine reference floors
$$\psi_j=\max(\psi,-j),\quad
u_j=F_D(\psi_j)=\max(u,\sigma-j),\quad
B_j=U\cap\{\psi>-j\}.\tag{6.5}$$
The bounded local metric product of $\nu_j$ exists by Section 6.1. Its restriction to $B_j$ increases with $j$: on overlapping strict winning sets, G0 genuine locality in the quotient gives equality, and $B_j$ increases. The sets are Borel; they need not be ordinary open sets. Extend the restricted measures by zero and set
$$\operatorname{MA}_D(u)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathbf1_{B_j}T_{\nu_j}^n.\tag{6.6}$$
This construction precedes any target measure. Each positive measure has mass at most $V_D$. Their increasing limit is a finite Radon measure: for any Borel set, approximate one late finite measure from inside by a compact set; monotonicity and finite limiting mass prove inner regularity of the limit. On a compact Hausdorff space finite inner regularity also gives the requisite outer regularity by complements. Thus (6.6) is the genuine reference-floor nonpluripolar extension, of mass at most $V_D$ for general $\psi$.

If $\psi\in\mathcal E^1(X,A)$, quotient locality gives
$$\mathbf1_{B_j}T_{\nu_j}^n
 =\mathbf1_{B_j}\operatorname{MA}_{\rm raw,A}(\psi).$$
The latter measure integrates $\psi$ and gives zero mass to $Z$ by Section 6.2. The union of $B_j$ has full measure. Consequently
$$\operatorname{MA}_D(F_D\psi)=\operatorname{MA}_{\rm raw,A}(\psi),\quad
\operatorname{MA}_D(F_D\psi)(X^{\rm an})=V_D.\tag{6.7}$$
This equality has been derived from actual local metric products, locality, and a polar test. It is not an alternative inverse measure definition.

For different data and finite-energy $\psi_i$, use the common complement of their $Z_i$, the simultaneous quotient floors, and strict winning sets $\bigcap_i\{\psi_i>-j\}$. Mixed locality follows from finite algebraic polarization of the diagonal locality identities. The limiting product is the positive genuine mixed product of the $\psi_i$, of mass $(A_1\cdots A_n)$, and gives no mass to their pole sets. Polarization has alternating coefficients; positivity here follows from the actual mixed product, not from calling the polarization coefficients positive.

For decreasing genuine relative nets $u_{i\beta}\downarrow u_i$ with finite-energy endpoints, Section 5 proves $Q_{D_i}u_{i\beta}\downarrow Q_{D_i}u_i$ everywhere. G0 and (6.7) prove weak convergence of all unbounded mixed measures with their fixed raw intersection masses. If $\psi_{0\beta}\downarrow\psi_0$ is a genuine ample test net and $\psi_0$ is integrable for the limiting mixed measure, the signed weighted pairing is
$$\int\psi_0\,\bigwedge_{i=1}^nT_{u_i}
=(c_1(A_0),\psi_0)\prod_{i=1}^n(c_1(A_i),\psi_i)
 -(c_1(A_0),0)\prod_{i=1}^n(c_1(A_i),\psi_i).\tag{6.8}$$
G0 decreasing pairing continuity, the fixed positive masses, and the integrable-weight convergence give weak convergence of the weighted measures. Formula (6.8) keeps the correct subtraction order. Finite-energy quotient tests are integrable by G0 mixed integrability. Differences are handled by their two terms, not by declaring a difference of decreasing nets decreasing. We make no extra assertion that $\sigma$ itself is integrable against every admissible target or product; the meaningful relative weight is its unique quotient.

# 7. Actual energy and the intrinsic target class

For bounded quotients, define energy by the genuine products already constructed in Section 6:
$$E_D(u)=\frac1{n+1}\sum_{j=0}^n\int Q_Du\,
T_u^j\wedge T_\sigma^{n-j}.\tag{7.1}$$
Every integral is a finite integral on the original $X$. Equation (6.7) identifies each mixed factor with the ample factor of $\psi=Q_Du$ and reference $0$. The usual ample intersection energy formula in G0 therefore gives $E_D(u)=E_{\rm raw,A}(\psi)$. This equality has its local geometric justification in Section 6.1 and its pole removal justification in Section 6.2; it is not an abstract assignment to a lift. For arbitrary $u$ define
$$E_D(u)=\lim_{j\to\infty}E_D(\max(u,\sigma-j))
=E_{\rm raw,A}(Q_Du)\in\mathbb R\cup\{-\infty\}.\tag{7.2}$$
Ample monotonicity and decreasing continuity prove independence of the chosen cofinal real reference floors. It follows that
$$\mathcal E_D^1=F_D(\mathcal E^1(X,A)),\quad
E_D(\sigma)=0,\quad E_D(u+c)=E_D(u)+V_Dc.\tag{7.3}$$
This supplies the actual finite-energy space, reference and mass explicitly. Concavity, monotonicity, and weak upper semicontinuity follow from the order homeomorphism and the actual G0 energy. All finite mixed energies and weighted identities retain the raw masses from Section 6. For normalized $u$, define $J_D(u)=-V_D^{-1}E_D(u)$, equal to the normalized ample $J$.

The quotient is intrinsic to $(L,\sigma)$: it is the unique actual psh extension of the scalar difference on the reference complement. Different choices of that extension are impossible by Section 4. Intrinsic dual energy is exactly (1.6). Put $\bar\mu=\mu/V_D$ only when $V_D>0$. The identities are
$$D_D(\mu)=V_D E_{\rm BJ,A}^{\vee}(\bar\mu),\quad
\operatorname{MA}_D(u)=V_D\operatorname{MA}_{\rm BJ,A}(Q_Du).\tag{7.4}$$
The variational objective is constant invariant precisely when $\mu(X^{\rm an})=V_D$. If the mass is different, translating $u$ makes the objective unbounded above. Thus the mass is part of the target condition, not a normalization convention applied afterwards.

G0 on the actual ample $A$ proves that finite (7.4) is equivalent to integrability of every actual $\mathcal E^1(X,A)$ potential. The bijection (7.3) makes this exactly the all-$\mathcal E_D^1$ quotient integrability assertion of Theorem 1. For clarity, the nontrivial direction in that ample criterion uses bounded-$J$ integrability, finite positive convex sums with unused coefficient on zero, fixed-ample decreasing closure, and the ample scaling estimate for $J$; it does not select a solution or restrict measure support. Those are all actual-ample statements explicitly contained in G0 and are applied only to $A$. The polar test in Section 6.2 then implies $\mu(Z)=0$ as a consequence of the intrinsic condition. Absolute integrability of $u$ or of $\sigma$ is not added: subtracting the fixed reference is the correct relative quantity. The condition is defined before the inverse equation and cannot be satisfied merely by declaring a measure to be an inverse image.

# 8. Every continuous original variation and attained equation

## 8.1. Outer energy retaining the reference

For a reference-dominated upper bounded original expression $u+tf$, retain its prescribed all-point quotient $Q_Du+tf$. Define the reference outer energy using continuous quotient majorants:
$$E_D^{\downarrow}(u+tf)
=\inf_{b\in C^0(X^{\rm an}),\ b\geq Q_Du+tf}
E_D^{\downarrow}(\sigma+b)
=E_{\rm raw,A}^{\downarrow}(Q_Du+tf).\tag{8.1}$$
Here $E_D^{\downarrow}(\sigma+b)=E_{\rm raw,A}(P_A(b))$ by (5.2). Continuous majorants form a decreasing directed approximation net to an upper bounded usc quotient on compact Hausdorff $X^{\rm an}$, and G0 outer energy gives the last equality. Equivalently it is the energy of the exact relative envelope of that original obstacle. At $Z$ the original scalar obstacle is $-\infty$, so literal subtraction would lose values; (8.1) records the uniquely retained quotient rather than choosing them arbitrarily. If a continuous $b$ only satisfies $\sigma+b\geq u+tf$ originally, its divisorial inequality implies $b\geq Q_Du+tf$ everywhere by the ample usc obstacle principle. Thus the same infimum is obtained from the original inequality itself. This makes (8.1) an intrinsic reference construction.

G0's actual-ample two-sided outer derivative applies to $\psi=Q_Du\in\mathcal E^1(X,A)$ and every continuous original $f$. Combining (8.1) and (6.7) proves (1.7). The extension from PL tests does not lose a sign: if $\|f-f_m\|_\infty\leq\epsilon_m$, then
$$\psi+tf_m-|t|\epsilon_m\leq\psi+tf\leq\psi+tf_m+|t|\epsilon_m.$$ 
Monotonicity and the raw translation factor bound the energy error by $V_D|t|\epsilon_m$. Apply the ample uniform PL derivative first, divide by positive or negative $t$ with the proper reversed inequality, and let $m\to\infty$. Both derivatives equal $\int f\,\operatorname{MA}_D(u)$. All continuous variations are on the original carrier; none is required to descend from a skeleton or modification.

## 8.2. Coercivity and an actual maximizer

Let $\mu$ satisfy (1.6), write $d=D_D(\mu)/V_D$, and take normalized actual $u_j\in\mathcal E_D^1$ whose raw objectives tend to $D_D(\mu)$. Their actual quotients $\psi_j=Q_Du_j$ satisfy $\sup\psi_j=0$. The ample strict-concavity coercivity estimate in G0 says
$$J(\psi_j)\leq C_n\left(J_{\bar\mu}(\psi_j)+d\right),\qquad
J_{\bar\mu}(\psi)=d-E_{\rm BJ,A}(\psi)+\int\psi\,d\bar\mu.\tag{8.2}$$
The second term tends to zero, so these $J$'s are bounded. The normalized compactness proved in Section 5 supplies a subnet converging to an actual normalized $u\in\mathcal P_D(L)$, with actual quotient $\psi$. Lower semicontinuity of $J$ gives $J(\psi)<\infty$, hence $u\in\mathcal E_D^1$. This is net compactness on potentially nonmetrizable spaces; the countability of a maximizing sequence does not assert a sequentially compact analytic space or make the field countable.

The ample bounded-$J$ target-integral continuity in G0 yields $\int\psi_jd\mu\to\int\psi d\mu$ on that subnet. Upper semicontinuity of raw ample energy, transported by (7.2), therefore gives
$$E_D(u)-\int Q_Du\,d\mu\geq D_D(\mu).\tag{8.3}$$
The reverse inequality is its defining supremum. This proves actual attainment by a genuine original function. We have not replaced this step by convergence of output measures, an abstract metric completion, or an arbitrary lift.

## 8.3. Both Euler inequalities and genuine existence

For $f\in C^0(X^{\rm an})$ and real $t$, the relative envelope of $u+tf$ corresponds to $P_A(\psi+tf)$, lies between $\psi\pm|t|\|f\|_\infty$ in its quotient, and is actual finite energy. Feasibility and maximality (8.3) give
$$E_D^{\downarrow}(u+tf)-\int(\psi+tf)\,d\mu
\leq E_D(u)-\int\psi\,d\mu.\tag{8.4}$$
Indeed the envelope's objective is at least the left side, since its quotient is no larger than the obstacle; it is at most the attained maximum. Differentiating (8.4) for $t>0$ gives $\int f\operatorname{MA}_D(u)\leq\int f d\mu$. For $t<0$, division reverses the inequality and gives the opposite inequality. Consequently the integrals agree for every continuous $f$. Riesz uniqueness on the compact original analytic space gives
$$\operatorname{MA}_D(u)=\mu.\tag{8.5}$$
The operator used here was the original local metric and reference-floor operator of Section 6, constructed before selecting $\mu$. Existence thus proves a genuine equation. Conversely the ample concavity inequality and (7.4) show that every finite-energy solution has finite dual energy and attains the dual supremum, so (1.6) is exactly the solved measure class.

# 9. Comparison, domination and uniqueness on the original carrier

For $u=F_D(\psi)$ and $v=F_D(\chi)$, on $U$ one has $u<v$ iff $\psi<\chi$. On $Z$ both original scalars are $-\infty$; the operator and every target in (1.6) give that set zero mass. G0 comparison in the actual ample quotient therefore gives precisely
$$\int_{\{u<v\}}\operatorname{MA}_D(v)
\leq\int_{\{u<v\}}\operatorname{MA}_D(u).\tag{9.1}$$
Strict winning locality follows the same way and was already used to construct reference-floor products. Mixed locality follows by the finite polarization justified in Section 6. Domination follows from the ample domination principle and order reflection: if $u\leq v$ almost everywhere for $\operatorname{MA}_D(v)$, the quotient inequality holds almost everywhere off $Z$, hence everywhere for the actual ample quotients, and $u\leq v$ originally at all points.

Define the original finite-energy distance functional by its actual relative differences:
$$I_D(u,v)=\int(\psi-\chi)
\left(\operatorname{MA}_D(v)-\operatorname{MA}_D(u)\right)
=V_DI_A(\psi,\chi)\geq0.\tag{9.2}$$
The quotient differences are integrable by G0; this does not subtract two original $-\infty$ values. If the two raw output measures coincide, $I_D=0$. Ample uniqueness gives $\psi-\chi=c$ at every point, and hence $u=v+c$ on all of $X^{\rm an}$. Conversely translating a metric preserves its products. Connected integral $X$ yields one constant. This proves the uniqueness claim in the actual cone, including the retained reference values.

# 10. Numerical zero foundations and the separate zero-mass range

Let $\phi\in\operatorname{PSH}_{\rm def}(0)$ have its one defining real FS net $\phi_i$ on $B_i$, $c_1(B_i)\to0$. Let $C_i=\phi_i(v_0)\downarrow C=\phi(v_0)\in\mathbb R$. Fix an actual ample $A$. Refine the defining indices by rational $\varepsilon>0$ for which
$$\varepsilon A-B_i\text{ is actual ample}.\tag{10.1}$$
For every $\varepsilon$, (10.1) holds eventually. The admissible pairs ordered by increasing $i$ and decreasing $\varepsilon$ are directed and final over the original indices, with $\varepsilon\to0$: choose a smaller $\varepsilon$ first, then an index far enough into its ample tail. This also works for a stationary original net.

Section multiplication by a generating family of a cleared power of (10.1) realizes
$$a_{i,\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-1}(\phi_i-C_i)
\in\operatorname{PSH}(X,A),\qquad a_{i,\varepsilon}(v_0)=0.\tag{10.2}$$
For each divisorial $v$, continuous evaluation on the compact normalized actual ample cone has a finite lower bound $M_v$. Thus
$$\varepsilon M_v\leq\phi_i(v)-C_i\leq0.\tag{10.3}$$
Passing to the refined limit gives $\phi(v)=C$ on every divisorial point. Section 3.2 puts $\phi$ into a fixed actual ample $H$; the constant $C$ is in that same cone. G0 divisorial determination proves $\phi=C$ at all analytic points. Conversely a constant is an actual trivial-bundle FS function. This proves $\operatorname{PSH}(0)=\mathbb R$ directly. It does not use closure of the sequence $m\phi$ in a varying or nonample class.

For the actual trivial bundle on $n\geq1$ dimensional $X$, a constant metric has curvature zero. Its genuine local and global $n$-fold products are zero. Set the reference to zero and define $\mathcal E^1_0=\mathbb R$, $E_0=0$, $\operatorname{MA}_0=0$. These are its actual metric products and intersection energy, since every positive-dimensional factor has class zero. The only positive target of raw mass zero is $\mu=0$, and its dual supremum is zero. Every constant is integrable and solves the equation; any two differ by a constant. The normalized cone is the singleton zero, comparison is $0\leq0$, and the outer energy in every continuous direction is zero. Envelopes of bounded scalar obstacles are the constants equal to their infima. No expression divides by $(0^n)$ or converts the zero measure into a probability. Dimension zero is excluded from this assertion: the empty product there has unit mass.

This range cannot be enlarged to all zero-volume pseudoeffective classes by the same uniqueness statement. On $X=\mathbb P^1\times\mathbb P^1$, $L=p_1^*\mathcal O(1)$ is semiample and nontrivial with $L^2=0$. For $b>0$, the actual finite FS potential
$$u_b=\max(\log\|s_0\|,\log\|s_1\|+b)-b$$
is normalized and nonconstant. Along the ray where $s_1$ vanishes and $s_0$ is a unit it equals $\max(0,b-t)-b$. Its actual metric is pulled back from the first factor. Smooth FS approximation has two-fold curvature product zero, and BE uniform continuity gives $\operatorname{MA}_{\rm raw,L}(u_b)=0$. Distinct $b$ yield normalized functions differing by more than constants. Thus zero measure alone cannot yield uniqueness modulo constants for a general zero-volume positivity datum. This is a mathematical obstruction to an unqualified extension, not a solution of the original axis.

# 11. Preserved failure, scope and unresolved statements

The actual108 independent verdict is preserved verbatim in the evidence. Its counterexample is also proved here. Let $K$ be the disjoint union of an isolated $q$ and a sequence $p_j\to p_\infty$, all $p_j$ isolated. Let $S=\mathbb N_{>0}$, $T=\mathbb R$, and $\rho(n)=1/n$. Let $F_n$ consist of extended-continuous functions with value $c$ at $q$, $-\infty$ at $p_n$, and $c-m$ elsewhere, for $m\geq1$ and real $c$. They are stable under constants. For fixed $n$, increasing $m$ gives a decreasing limit $g$ equal to zero at $q$ and $-\infty$ everywhere else. Thus the constant outer family $g_n=g$ with $\rho(n)\to0$ meets the proposed varying-family closure hypotheses.

Yet no decreasing net of original family members can have $\rho\to0$. After any term from $F_n$, all later terms must remain in $F_n$, because they must keep the value $-\infty$ at $p_n$, and every other family is finite there. Its $\rho$ is fixed on that entire tail. Therefore the closure at zero is empty. This disproves BJ v4 Lemma 4.6 as stated. The printed strict shift at a common pole would read $-\infty+\epsilon<-\infty+\epsilon'$, which is false; its exponential difference is zero. Sections 3--4 and 10 do not use that argument or a hidden varying-class closure behind BJ 4.15, 6.17, 6.24 or 6.31. The lemma's failure does not disprove Theorem 1. The preserved public renderer coordinates are BJ v4 L3187--3198@P45 for its statement, L3222--3241@P45--46 for the defective directedness argument, L3264--3270@P46 for its closure application, L3550@P49 for the invocation in 4.15(i), and L4559--4565@P65 for the invocation of 4.15 in 6.17.

The full-big conclusion proved in this paper is an actual equation theorem for the entire reference-dominated cone for every fixed $L=A+D$ datum, with all targets of intrinsic finite energy and raw mass $(A^n)$. It has no countability, toric, skeletal, support, or finite-generation restriction. Every construction occurs on the original smooth projective $X$. The intrinsic reference is genuinely retained. Theorem 2 supplies the separately stated trivial-bundle zero-volume endpoint.

The unchanged original problem is broader if it asks for a class-level equation over the general pseudoeffective nonample domain or for the minimal-singularity big cone of mass $\operatorname{vol}(L)$. Neither follows from this paper. Concrete remaining mathematical statements are:

- For an arbitrary big rational $L$, construct and prove the equation theory on its minimal-singularity or another generally prescribed model-reference cone beyond the divisorial references whose residual bundle is actually ample, with its intrinsic mass (in the minimal-singularity case, $\operatorname{vol}(L)$).
- For arbitrary nonbig pseudoeffective nontrivial rational data, identify the correct intrinsic energy, zero or lower-dimensional measure datum and an appropriate uniqueness formulation; prove the original-carrier operator, compactness, envelopes, differentiation, comparison and attainment. Ordinary positive zero-mass measures alone cannot retain uniqueness modulo constants, as Section 10 shows.
- If the intended full original theorem includes all reference choices or all of $\operatorname{PSH}(L)$ with one class-intrinsic mass, prove compatibility and the necessary replacement inputs for those larger cones. No such compatibility has been asserted between distinct $(A,D)$ choices here.

Accordingly the canonical result is marked **candidate=false** for the complete unchanged original P16.5, while retaining Theorems 1--2 and the direct foundation repairs as substantive new deductions relative to G0. This conservative scope assessment prevents an entire prescribed-reference theorem from being advertised as a general pseudoeffective solution. No further mathematical gap is intentionally assumed inside the stated prescribed-reference theorem; it remains an unverified draft with the exact external premises listed below.

# Abstract

We establish the retained domain of original P16.1: reduced irreducible geometrically unibranch projective varieties over an arbitrary algebraically closed trivially valued field of characteristic zero, polarized by an actual ample rational line bundle. The conclusion concerns the genuine intersection-theoretic finite-energy Monge--Ampère operator and all positive Radon targets of its intersection mass and finite intrinsic dual energy, with arbitrary support. The new deduction is original-target envelope regularity. We prove the application joining the specified accepted all-point fiber theorem, actual-reference local conversion, geometric witness construction, and repaired countable original-ring affine theorem. First genuine membership on the original variety precedes envelope regularity and every use of onto birational descent. A separate countable field for each finite competitor removes countability. The general-variety energy theory then gives an actually attained variational maximizer and its equation, with both derivative signs. Normalization, mixed products and unbounded limits preserve the original cycle and raw mass.

This is an unverified research draft. Its mathematical premises include exactly the accepted intermediate bodies supplied with the assignment, in the scopes stated below. Their previous acceptance is a task grant, not a claim of a new independent review. The new application and the final theorem are proved here relative to those grants. Compilation and provenance checks have no mathematical force.

# 1. Statement, conventions and premises

Throughout, $k$ is algebraically closed, has characteristic zero, and has the trivial absolute value. There is no countability assumption unless explicitly imposed in Sections 3--5. A variety is reduced and of finite type. The target $X$ is irreducible, geometrically unibranch, and projective over $k$. It may be nonnormal and singular. Its dimension is $n$. The polarization $L$ is an **actual ample rational line bundle**: a positive integral multiple is an honest ample line bundle. Put $\omega=c_1(L)$ and $V=(L^n)>0$. Intersections use the fundamental cycle. A rational bundle is cleared by tensor powers whenever sections or models occur. Analytic spaces and Radon measures are Berkovich spaces and regular Borel measures. Potentials are scalar differences from the canonical trivial metric on the specified bundle, with values in $\mathbb R\cup\{-\infty\}$.

The genuine space $\operatorname{PSH}(X,L)$ is the space of generically finite psh potentials in the sense of Boucksom--Jonsson (BJ). For ample rational $L$, it is the decreasing-net closure of finite Fubini--Study potentials on that same bundle. Its topology is pointwise convergence on divisorial valuations of $k(X)/k$. This topology is called weak below; it is not convergence at every analytic point. Write $\operatorname{PSH}_{\sup}$ for $\sup_X\varphi=0$. For irreducible $X$, the supremum equals the value at the generic trivial valuation. Finite maxima, constants, genuine pullback and decreasing varying-class closure have their usual BJ meanings.

**Theorem 1 (original retained P16.1).** For every $k,X,L$ just specified, $\omega$ has the envelope property, and $\operatorname{PSH}_{\sup}(X,L)$ is compact in the divisorial topology. There are symmetric positive raw mixed Radon measures
$$
 (\omega_1+dd^c\varphi_1)\wedge\cdots\wedge(\omega_n+dd^c\varphi_n)
$$
for actual ample rational $L_i$ and genuine $\varphi_i\in\mathcal E^1(X,L_i)$, of mass $(L_1\cdots L_n)$, continuous along unbounded decreasing nets with genuine finite-energy limits. In particular
$$
 \operatorname{MA}_{\rm raw,L}(\varphi)=(\omega+dd^c\varphi)^n,
 \qquad \operatorname{MA}_{\rm raw,L}(\varphi)(X^{\rm an})=V.
$$
Define
$$
 E_{\rm raw}(\varphi)=\frac{(\omega,\varphi)^{n+1}}{n+1},\quad
 E_{\rm BJ}=V^{-1}E_{\rm raw},\quad
 \mathcal E^1(X,L)=\{\varphi\in\operatorname{PSH}(X,L):E_{\rm raw}(\varphi)>-\infty\}.
$$
Every positive Radon $\mu$ with $\mu(X^{\rm an})=V$ and
$$
 E_{\rm raw}^{\vee}(\mu)
 :=\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal E^1(X,L)}
       \left(E_{\rm raw}(\varphi)-\int\varphi\,d\mu\right)<\infty
 \tag{1.1}
$$
has a solution $\varphi_\mu\in\mathcal E^1(X,L)$ of
$$\operatorname{MA}_{\rm raw,L}(\varphi_\mu)=\mu.$$
It is unique modulo one real constant. Condition (1.1) is equivalent to integrability of **every genuine** $\mathcal E^1(X,L)$ potential against $\mu$. There is no imposed support condition. The raw outer energy satisfies
$$
 \left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0} E_{\rm raw}^{\downarrow}(\varphi+tf)
       =\int f\,\operatorname{MA}_{\rm raw,L}(\varphi)
 \tag{1.2}
$$
for $\varphi\in\mathcal E^1$ and $f\in C^0(X^{\rm an})$. Finite-energy locality, comparison and domination hold on the original $X$.

For $n=0$, $X=\operatorname{Spec}k$ is literally one point, $V=1$, $E_{\rm raw}(a)=a$, and the empty product is the unit point measure. All finite scalar potentials are solutions; their differences are constants. The arguments involving curves, $I$, or positive-dimensional products below are used only for $n\ge1$.

The theorem retains projectivity, an ample actual rational line bundle, algebraizability, characteristic zero, algebraic closedness and the trivial valuation. It makes no assertion about the other P16 axes, or about nonunibranch singular targets. Arbitrary support here means all measures satisfying the intrinsic condition (1.1); integrability itself excludes prohibited singular mass.

## 1.1. Exact accepted intermediate mathematical assumptions

We use the following supplied theorems. Their names describe mathematical statements, rather than importing an application from a report label. The complete supplied proof bodies are preserved verbatim in the evidence bundle. The binding strings identify which body and clarification are assumed.

Bounded local decreasing contact limits admit genuine collars with the actual reference, positive raw local products, compact signed integration by parts, mass, comparison and local domination. With genuine EP, these local potentials are genuinely psh. The smooth upper-growth and principal-open removal consequences retain smooth ample-boundary completion and projective birational domination of the **one original witness**, and apply only to the exact unit-log same-constant contact class. **Repaired countable original-ring theorem.** Let $k$ be countable in the present field domain and $U=\operatorname{Spec}R$ integral affine. Suppose one proper birational integral witness pulls $u$ back locally to decreasing rational contact sequences on common ordinary neighborhoods. A contact has finitely many unit-log affine coordinates, lies below its weight, and agrees at the tested point with the **same additive constant**; floors handle zero branches. Suppose each specified smooth affine operation has the geometric completions and dominations of the preceding theorem, retaining the restricted witness and actual references. Then $u$ has decreasing original-$R$ affine FS regularization, and strict sublevels are Runge. Its projective consequence uses the **original canonical reference of an actual ample rational bundle**. The complete whole-base trace, controlled approximation, countable barrier, Rossi, Runge, Hartogs and singular-stratum body is granted with its repair.
