Notation and conventions.
     • We use the standard abbreviations usc for ‘upper semicontinuous’, lsc for ‘lower
       semicontinuous’, wlog for ‘without loss of generality”, and iff for ‘if and only if’.
       We also use PL for ‘piecewise linear’, snc for ‘simple normal crossing’, and psh for
       ‘plurisubharmonic’.
     • Following the Bourbaki convention, all compact and locally compact topological
       spaces are required to be Hausdorff.
     • A net in a set X is a family (xi )i∈I of elements of X indexed by a directed set, i.e.
       a partially preordered set in which any two elements are dominated by a third one.
       A subnet of (xi )i∈I is a net of the form (xϕ(j) )j∈J where ϕ : J → I is increasing and
       final, i.e. for each i ∈ I there exists j ∈ J with ϕ(j) ≥ i. A (Hausdorff) topological
       space X is compact iff every net in X admits a convergent subnet.
     • If X is a Hausdorff topological space, and ϕ : X → R ∪ {±∞} is any function, then
       the usc regularization ϕ? of ϕ is the smallest usc function with ϕ? ≥ ϕ. Concretely,
       ϕ? (x) = lim supy→x ϕ(y). The lsc regularization is defined by ϕ? = −(−ϕ)? .
     • We work over an algebraically closed field k, for the most part of arbitrary charac-
       teristic. In this paper, a variety (over k) is a separated k-scheme of finite type that
       is reduced, but not necessarily irreducible, nor even equidimensional.
10                      SÉBASTIEN BOUCKSOM AND MATTIAS JONSSON

     • For any variety X, we denote by Pic(X) the Picard group of isomorphism classes of
       line bundles. Elements of the associated Q-vector space Pic(X)Q := Pic(X) ⊗Z Q
       can be viewed as isomorphism classes of Q-line bundles.
     • If X is a projective variety, we denote by N1 (X) the finite dimensional R-vector
       space of numerical classes of R-Cartier divisors on X. It comes with a surjective
       linear map Pic(X)R → N1 (X) induced by L 7→ c1 (L) .
     • Ample classes form a nonempty open convex cone Amp(X) ⊂ N1 (X), whose closure
       is the closed convex cone Nef(X) ⊂ N1 (X) of nef classes. For θ, θ0 ∈ N1 (X), we
       write θ ≥ θ0 if θ − θ0 is nef. We generally denote by θ an element of N1 (X), and by
       ω an element of Amp(X).
     • The cone Psef(X) ⊂ N1 (X) of pseudoeffective classes is defined as the closed convex
       cone generated by classes of effective R-Cartier divisors; its interior Big(X) is the
       cone of big classes.
     • A section of a line bundle on X is regular if it does not vanish identically along any
       irreducible component of X. Its zero scheme is then a Cartier divisor on X.
END MATHEMATICAL DATA

  4.1. The class of θ-psh functions. Recall that:
       • a function ϕ ∈ X an → R ∪ {−∞} is called generically finite if ϕ|Xαan 6≡ −∞ for all α
         (see Definition 1.3);
                                gf
       • for any L ∈ Pic(X)Q , HR  (L) denotes the set of generically finite (continuous) func-
                    an
         tions ϕ : X → R ∪ {−∞} of the form
                                    ϕ = m−1 max{log |si | + λi }
                                                i
           where (si ) is a finite set of sections of mL with m sufficiently divisible, λi ∈ R (see
           Definition 2.18). This set is non-empty iff L is effective.
  Given any numerical class θ ∈ N1 (X), we are seeking to define a class PSH(θ) of θ-
  plurisubharmonic functions (θ-psh functions for short) ϕ : X an → R ∪ {−∞}. By analogy
  with the complex analytic setting, these functions should be usc and generically finite, and
  the following properties should hold:
                                                gf
 (PSH1) for any L ∈ Pic(X)Q , we have HR           (L) ⊂ PSH(L) := PSH(c1 (L));
 (PSH2) if a generically finite function ϕ : X an → R ∪ {−∞} arises as the pointwise limit of
           a decreasing net (ϕi ) with ϕi ∈ PSH(θi ) and limi θi = θ, then ϕ ∈ PSH(θ).
  Note that (PSH2) implies the following two properties:
(PSH2a) for any function ϕ : X an → R ∪ {−∞}, the set of θ ∈ N1 (X) such that ϕ ∈ PSH(θ)
           is closed (possibly empty);
(PSH2b) for any θ ∈ N1 (X), the set PSH(θ) is closed under decreasing limits (provided the
           limit function is generically finite).
  Taking (PSH1) and (PSH2) as minimal requirements directly leads to the following defini-
  tion:
  Definition 4.1. For any θ ∈ N1 (X), we define a θ-psh function ϕ : X an → R ∪ {−∞} as a
  generically finite, usc function that can be written as the pointwise limit of a decreasing net

  ϕi ∈ HR   (Li ) with Li ∈ Pic(X)Q such that limi c1 (Li ) = θ in N1 (X).

Proposition 28.27.13. Let $X$ be a quasi-compact scheme. Let $\mathcal{L}$ be an invertible sheaf on $X$. Set $S = \Gamma _*(X, \mathcal{L})$. The following are equivalent: 
  
  
 $\mathcal{L}$ is ample, 


 the open sets $X_ s$, with $s \in S_{+}$ homogeneous, cover $X$ and the associated morphism $X \to \text{Proj}(S)$ is an open immersion, 


 the open sets $X_ s$, with $s \in S_{+}$ homogeneous, form a basis for the topology of $X$, 


 the open sets $X_ s$, with $s \in S_{+}$ homogeneous, which are affine form a basis for the topology of $X$, 


 for every quasi-coherent sheaf $\mathcal{F}$ on $X$ the sum of the images of the canonical maps 


  \[  \Gamma (X, \mathcal{F} \otimes _{\mathcal{O}_ X} \mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}) \otimes _{\mathbf{Z}} \mathcal{L}^{\otimes -n} \longrightarrow \mathcal{F}  \]


 with $n \geq 1$ equals $\mathcal{F}$, 


 same property as (5) with $\mathcal{F}$ ranging over all quasi-coherent sheaves of ideals, 


 $X$ is quasi-separated and for every quasi-coherent sheaf $\mathcal{F}$ of finite type on $X$ there exists an integer $n_0$ such that $\mathcal{F} \otimes _{\mathcal{O}_ X} \mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}$ is globally generated for all $n \geq n_0$, 


 $X$ is quasi-separated and for every quasi-coherent sheaf $\mathcal{F}$ of finite type on $X$ there exist integers $n > 0$, $k \geq 0$ such that $\mathcal{F}$ is a quotient of a direct sum of $k$ copies of $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes - n}$, and 


 same as in (8) with $\mathcal{F}$ ranging over all sheaves of ideals of finite type on $X$. 




    
      Proof.
      Lemma 28.27.11 is (1) $\Rightarrow $ (2). Lemmas 28.27.2 and 28.27.12 provide the implication (1) $\Leftarrow $ (2). The implications (2) $\Rightarrow $ (4) $\Rightarrow $ (3) are clear from Constructions, Section 27.8. Lemma 28.27.6 is (3) $\Rightarrow $ (1). Thus we see that the first 4 conditions are all equivalent. 
    
      Assume the equivalent conditions (1) – (4). Note that in particular $X$ is separated (as an open subscheme of the separated scheme $\text{Proj}(S)$). Let $\mathcal{F}$ be a quasi-coherent sheaf on $X$. Choose $s \in S_{+}$ homogeneous such that $X_ s$ is affine. We claim that any section $m \in \Gamma (X_ s, \mathcal{F})$ is in the image of one of the maps displayed in (5) above. This will imply (5) since these affines $X_ s$ cover $X$. Namely, by Lemma 28.18.2 we may write $m$ as the image of $m' \otimes s^{-n}$ for some $n \geq 1$, some $m' \in \Gamma (X, \mathcal{F} \otimes \mathcal{L}^{\otimes n})$. This proves the claim. 
    
      Clearly (5) $\Rightarrow $ (6). Let us assume (6) and prove $\mathcal{L}$ is ample. Pick $x \in X$. Let $U \subset X$ be an affine open which contains $x$. Set $Z = X \setminus U$. We may think of $Z$ as a reduced closed subscheme, see Schemes, Section 26.12. Let $\mathcal{I} \subset \mathcal{O}_ X$ be the quasi-coherent sheaf of ideals corresponding to the closed subscheme $Z$. By assumption (6), there exists an $n \geq 1$ and a section $s \in \Gamma (X, \mathcal{I} \otimes \mathcal{L}^{\otimes n})$ such that $s$ does not vanish at $x$ (more precisely such that $s \not\in \mathfrak m_ x \mathcal{I}_ x \otimes \mathcal{L}_ x^{\otimes n}$). We may think of $s$ as a section of $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}$. Since it clearly vanishes along $Z$ we see that $X_ s \subset U$. Hence $X_ s$ is affine, see Lemma 28.27.4. This proves that $\mathcal{L}$ is ample. At this point we have proved that (1) – (6) are equivalent. 
    
      Assume the equivalent conditions (1) – (6). In the following we will use the fact that the tensor product of two sheaves of modules which are globally generated is globally generated without further mention (see Modules, Lemma 17.4.3). By (1) we can find elements $s_ i \in S_{d_ i}$ with $d_ i \geq 1$ such that $X = \bigcup _{i = 1, \ldots , n} X_{s_ i}$. Set $d = d_1\ldots d_ n$. It follows that $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes d}$ is globally generated by 
    
      
  \[  s_1^{d/d_1}, \ldots , s_ n^{d/d_ n}.  \]

    
       This means that if $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes j}$ is globally generated then so is $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes j + dn}$ for all $n \geq 0$. Fix a $j \in \{ 0, \ldots , d - 1\} $. For any point $x \in X$ there exists an $n \geq 1$ and a global section $s$ of $\mathcal{L}^{j + dn}$ which does not vanish at $x$, as follows from (5) applied to $\mathcal{F} = \mathcal{L}^{\otimes j}$ and ample invertible sheaf $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes d}$. Since $X$ is quasi-compact there we may find a finite list of integers $n_ i$ and global sections $s_ i$ of $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes j + dn_ i}$ which do not vanish at any point of $X$. Since $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes d}$ is globally generated this means that $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes j + dn}$ is globally generated where $n = \max \{ n_ i\} $. Since we proved this for every congruence class mod $d$ we conclude that there exists an $n_0 = n_0(\mathcal{L})$ such that $\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}$ is globally generated for all $n \geq n_0$. At this point we see that if $\mathcal{F}$ is globally generated then so is $\mathcal{F} \otimes \mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}$ for all $n \geq n_0$. 
    
      We continue to assume the equivalent conditions (1) – (6). Let $\mathcal{F}$ be a quasi-coherent sheaf of $\mathcal{O}_ X$-modules of finite type. Denote $\mathcal{F}_ n \subset \mathcal{F}$ the image of the canonical map of (5). By construction $\mathcal{F}_ n \otimes \mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}$ is globally generated. By (5) we see $\mathcal{F}$ is the sum of the subsheaves $\mathcal{F}_ n$, $n \geq 1$. By Modules, Lemma 17.9.7 we see that $\mathcal{F} = \sum _{n = 1, \ldots , N} \mathcal{F}_ n$ for some $N \geq 1$. It follows that $\mathcal{F} \otimes \mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}$ is globally generated whenever $n \geq N + n_0(\mathcal{L})$ with $n_0(\mathcal{L})$ as above. We conclude that (1) – (6) implies (7). 
    
      Assume (7). Let $\mathcal{F}$ be a quasi-coherent sheaf of $\mathcal{O}_ X$-modules of finite type. By (7) there exists an integer $n \geq 1$ such that the canonical map 
    
      
  \[  \Gamma (X, \mathcal{F} \otimes _{\mathcal{O}_ X} \mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}) \otimes _{\mathbf{Z}} \mathcal{L}^{\otimes -n} \longrightarrow \mathcal{F}  \]

    
       is surjective. Let $I$ be the set of finite subsets of $\Gamma (X, \mathcal{F} \otimes _{\mathcal{O}_ X} \mathcal{L}^{\otimes n})$ partially ordered by inclusion. Then $I$ is a directed partially ordered set. For $i = \{ s_1, \ldots , s_{r(i)}\} $ let $\mathcal{F}_ i \subset \mathcal{F}$ be the image of the map 
    
      
  \[  \bigoplus \nolimits _{j = 1, \ldots , r(i)} \mathcal{L}^{\otimes -n} \longrightarrow \mathcal{F}  \]

    
       which is multiplication by $s_ j$ on the $j$th factor. The surjectivity above implies that $\mathcal{F} = \mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\nolimits _{i \in I} \mathcal{F}_ i$. Hence Modules, Lemma 17.9.7 applies and we conclude that $\mathcal{F} = \mathcal{F}_ i$ for some $i$. Hence we have proved (8). In other words, (7) $\Rightarrow $ (8). 
    
      The implication (8) $\Rightarrow $ (9) is trivial. 
    
      Finally, assume (9). Let $\mathcal{I} \subset \mathcal{O}_ X$ be a quasi-coherent sheaf of ideals. By Lemma 28.23.3 (this is where we use the condition that $X$ be quasi-separated) we see that $\mathcal{I} = \mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\nolimits _\alpha I_\alpha $ with each $I_\alpha $ quasi-coherent of finite type. Since by assumption each of the $I_\alpha $ is a quotient of negative tensor powers of $\mathcal{L}$ we conclude the same for $\mathcal{I}$ (but of course without the finiteness or boundedness of the powers). Hence we conclude that (9) implies (6). This ends the proof of the proposition. 
      $\square$

END MATHEMATICAL DATA

7.2. Nef metrics. Assume that K is non-Archimedean. A model metric φ on a Q-line
bundle L is nef if φ = φL for some Q-model L of L which is nef, i.e. L · C ≥ 0 for each
e
K-projective  curve C ⊂ Xs . By [GM19, Proposition 3.5], any Q-model L′ of L such that
φ = φL′ is then also nef.
Definition 7.7. A nef metric on a Q-line bundle L is a continuous metric φ that can be
written as a uniform limit of nef model metrics.
   This is usually known as a semipositive metric in the sense of S.W. Zhang, cf. [GM19] for
a thorough discussion. When K is trivially valued, the trivial metric is the only nef metric.
Theorem 7.8. Assume that K is nontrivially valued, and let L be an ample Q-line bundle.
Then a continuous metric on L is nef iff it is psh (in the sense of Definition 7.2).
   A proof of this result is also sketched, in a slightly different language, in [CMor18, Remark
3.18]; we nevertheless provide a proof below, for completeness. By [GM19, Corollary 1.4],
we get:
Corollary 7.9. Let L be an ample Q-line bundle, and pick a Q-model L of L. Then φL is
psh iff L is nef.
Proof of Theorem 7.8. By Theorem 5.14, pure Fubini–Study metrics on L coincide with
model metrics associated to Q-models L of L that are semiample, and hence nef. Since K
is nontrivially valued, Corollary 7.6 implies that every continuous psh metric is a uniform
limit of pure Fubini–Study metrics, and hence is nef.
   To prove the converse, let L be a nef Q-model of L, determined on a model X of X.
Using again Theorem 5.14, it will be enough to show that φL is a uniform limit of model
metrics associated to semiample Q-models. Since L is ample, it admits an ample Q-model
A on some projective model X ′ dominating X (cf. [GM19, Lemma 4.12]). The pull-back
L′ of L to X ′ is nef. For each ε ∈ (0, 1) ∩ Q, Lε := (1 − ε)L′ + εH is thus ample on Xs ,
and hence on X , by [EGA, IV.9.6.4]. In particular, Lε is a semiample model of L. Since
φL = φL′ (Lemma 5.10), φLε − φL = ε(φH − φL ) converges to 0 uniformly as ε > 0, and we
are done.                                                                                 
Remark 7.10. We expect Theorem 7.8 and Corollary 7.9 to hold when L is merely semi-
ample. For this, it would be enough to show that any nef model metric on L descends to the
Stein factorization A as in Lemma 5.5; this would for instance follow from a relative version
of the local Hodge index theorem proved in [YZ16, Theorem 2.1].
7.3. Psh-regularizable metrics. The curvature form of a smooth metric φ on a line bun-
dle L is a closed (1, 1)-form, which we denote by ddc φ (see [CLD12, §6.4.1] for the non-
Archimedean case). For each trivializing section τ of L over an open U ⊂ X, the function
log |τ |φ is smooth, and ddc φ = −ddc log |τ |φ on U an . Again, this makes sense for Q-line
bundles as well.
Definition 7.11. A smooth metric φ on a Q-line bundle L is semipositive if its curvature
form ddc φ is semipositive (see [CLD12, §5.4] for the non-Archimedean case).

Definition 7.13. A (possibly singular) metric φ on a line bundle L is psh-regularizable if
φ can be written as the limit of a decreasing net φi of smooth semipositive metrics.
     If φ is continuous then φi → φ uniformly, by Dini’s lemma.

18.1.4. — En géométrie complexe, il est souvent important d’utiliser des métriques
singulières. Les constructions précédentes s’étendent naturellement dans ce cadre, pour
peu qu’on considère des semi-métriques localement intégrables.
   Soit X un n-espace k-analytique sans bord et soit L un fibré en droites sur X muni
d’une semi-métrique. On dit que cette semi-métrique est localement intégrable si pour
toute section inversible s de L sur un ouvert U de X, la fonction log∥s∥ sur U (à valeurs
dans R ∪ {−∞}) est localement intégrable.
   Par analogie avec la définition 17.8.1, on dit que cette semi-métrique est psh si, pour
toute section inversible s de L sur un ouvert U de X, la fonction log∥s∥ sur U est psh.
   Dans les deux cas, il suffit qu’il existe, au voisinage de tout point de X, une section
inversible de ce type, car les fonctions du type log|f |, pour une fonction analytique
inversible f , sont continues et psh.
                                                                               ×
   On déduit de même une section globale du faisceau quotient L1loc,X / log(|OX  |), respec-
                                                ×
tivement du faisceau quotient PshX / log(|OX |), ainsi que le courant de courbure c1 (L)
du fibré en droites semi-métrisé L, à semi-métrique localement intégrable. Lorsque cette
semi-métrique est psh, ce courant est positif.
18.1.5. — Soit X un n-espace k-analytique sans bord et soit L un fibré en droites
sur X. Comme en géométrie algébrique ([72], 20.1.8), le MX -module L ⊗OX MX est
inversible ; ses sections sont appelées sections méromorphes ; ses sections inversibles sont
appelées sections méromorphes régulières.
  Si s est une section méromorphe (régulière) de L, alors pour tout ouvert U et toute
section inversible ε de L sur U , le quotient s|U /ε est une fonction méromorphe (inversible)
sur U .
  Une section méromorphe régulière s possède un diviseur div(s), construit localement
dans des cartes trivialisantes. Son support est un fermé de Zariski purement de codi-
mension 1, sur le complémentaire U duquel s fournit une trivialisation de L.
  Supposons maintenant que L soit muni d’une semi-métrique localement intégrable.
Alors la fonction log∥s∥−1 est définie et localement intégrable sur U ; d’après le para-
graphe 7.1.12, elle définit alors un courant sur X que l’on note [log∥s∥−1 ].
Théorème 18.1.6 (Formule de Poincaré–Lelong). — Soit X un n-espace k-
analytique sans bord et soit L un fibré en droites sur X muni d’une semi-métrique
localement intégrable. Soit s une section méromorphe régulière de L sur X. On a
l’égalité
                            d′ d′′ [log∥s∥−1 ] + δdiv(s) = c1 (L).
Démonstration. — Cette égalité de courants se vérifie localement, sur un ouvert U sur
lequel L possède une section inversible ε. Soit f l’unique fonction méromorphe inversible
sur U telle que s|U = f ε. La formule indiquée résulte alors des définitions et de la formule
de Poincaré–Lelong (théorème 16.7.3) pour les fonctions méromorphes régulières.

Corollaire 18.1.7. — Soit X une k-courbe analytique propre et soit L un fibré en
droites sur X muni d’une semi-métrique localement intégrable. La masse totale du cou-
rant c1 (L) est égale au degré du fibré en droite L.
   Rappelons que X est algébrisable, de même que L, plus précisément qu’il existe, à
isomorphisme près, une unique k-courbe algébrique muni d’un fibré en droite redonnant
(X, L) après analytification. (Pour une démonstration de l’algébrisation d’une courbe,

voir [56], théorème 3.7.2. L’algébrisation de L et la propriété d’unicité se déduisent alors

tion détaillée.)
Démonstration. — Commençons par traiter le cas où X est intègre. Le fibré en droites L

                   § 8.8. EXTENSION AUX DIFFÉRENCES DE FONCTIONS PSH                           211

     § 8.8. EXTENSION AUX DIFFÉRENCES DE FONCTIONS PSH

Lemme 8.8.1. — Soit u une fonction lisse sur X. Tout point x de X possède un
voisinage sur lequel u s’écrit comme la différence de deux fonctions lisses psh.
   Plus précisément, soit U un ouvert contenu dans un domaine compact W sur lequel u
est tropicalisée par une carte tropicale f , il existe des fonctions lisses psh u1 , u2 sur U ,
f -tropicales, telles que u|U = u1 − u2 .

Démonstration. — Soit f : W → T un moment tropicalisant u, soit P le polyèdre com-
pact f (W ) et soit v une fonction lisse au voisinage de P telle que u|W = v ◦ ftrop .
   D’après la proposition 1.10.2, il existe des fonctions lisses v1 et v2 au voisinage de P
telles que v = v1 −v2 et telles que les formes d′ d′′ v1 et d′ d′′ v2 sont positives. Les fonctions
u1 = v1 ◦ ftrop |U et v2 = u2 ◦ ftrop |U sur U sont lisses, psh, et l’on a u = u1 − u2 .

Proposition 8.8.2. — a) Soit u une fonction G-lisse sur X. Soit W un domaine
analytique compact W sur lequel u est tropicalisée par un moment f , il existe des fonc-
tions convexes v1 , v2 sur l’enveloppe convexe de f (W ) telles que u|U = f ∗ v1 − f ∗ v2 .
   b) Soit u une fonction paralinéaire sur X et soit W un domaine analytique compact
de X sur lequel u est tropicalisée par un moment f . Il existe des fonctions paralinéaires
convexes v1 , v2 sur l’enveloppe convexe de f (W ) telles que u|U = f ∗ v1 − f ∗ v2 .

Démonstration. — D’après la proposition 6.3.4, il existe un voisinage domanial com-
pact V de x, une carte tropicale f : V → E sur V et une fonction G-lisse v sur f (V )
telle que u|V = f ∗ v. Lorsque u est paralinéaire, v l’est. D’après les propositions 1.10.10
et 1.10.11, il existe alors des fonctions convexes v1 et v2 sur l’enveloppe convexe de f (V )
telles que v = v1 − v2 sur f (V ), que l’on peut choisir paralinéaires sur u l’est. D’après la
proposition 8.2.5, les fonctions u1 = f ∗ v1 et u2 = f ∗ v2 sur V sont tropicalement convexes
et sont paralinéaires si u l’est ; elles vérifient u = u1 − u2 .

Corollaire 8.8.3. — Soit u une fonction G-lisse sur X. Tout point de X en lequel X
est décent possède un voisinage sur lequel u s’écrit comme la différence de deux fonctions
tropicalement convexes, que l’on peut choisir paralinéaires si u l’est.

8.8.4. — Soit CPsh±   X le sous-faisceau en R-espaces vectoriels de CX engendré par le
faisceau CPshX des fonctions psh. Ses sections seront appelées dpsh (« différence de
fonctions plurisousharmoniques »). D’après le lemme 8.8.1, ce sous-faisceau contient les
fonctions lisses. De même, les fonctions localement différence de deux fonctions tropi-
calement convexes seront appelées tropicalement d-convexes ; de telles fonctions sont
dpsh. Lorsque X est décent, toute fonction G-lisse est tropicalement d-convexe (propo-
sition 8.8.2).
8.8.5. — Soit T un courant positif fermé sur X de bidegré (q, q). Il existe un unique
morphisme (p + 1)-linéaire sur CX0 × (CPsh± p
                                          X) ,

                         (u0 , u1 , . . . , up ) 7→ u0 d′ d′′ u1 ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ up ,
à valeurs dans le faisceau des courants de bidegré (p + q, p + q) sur X qui coïncide
avec cette application lorsque u0 , u1 , . . . , up sont psh et u0 est positive. Par construc-
tion, l’image de ce faisceau est constituée de différences de deux courants positifs. Il est
212                         CHAPITRE 8. THÉORIE DE BEDFORD–TAYLOR

symétrique en (u1 , . . . , up ). Si T est de Bedford–Taylor, il ne dépend que de u0 et des
courants d′ d′′ u1 , . . . , d′ d′′ up . Si, de plus, u0 est dpsh, on a
             d′ d′′ (u0 d′ d′′ u1 ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ up ) = d′ d′′ u0 ∧ d′ d′′ u1 ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ up .
  Soit f 1 , . . . , f p des fonctions lisses telles que d′ d′′ (f i ) = 0. On a
      u0 d′ d′′ (u1 + f 1 ) ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ (up + f p ) ∧ T = u0 d′ d′′ (u1 ) ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ (up ) ∧ T,
même si T n’est pas de Bedford–Taylor. Autrement dit, ce morphisme passe au quotient
en un morphisme de source CX0 × (CPsh±                 p
                                            X / HarmX ) où HarmX est le faisceau sur X des
fonctions lisses h telles que d′ d′′ h = 0.
Remarque 8.8.6. — Supposons que u1 , . . . , up sont tropicalement d-convexes et soit α
une forme lisse de type (n − p, n − p) sur X. Alors u0 7→ ⟨u0 d′ d′′ u1 ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ up , α⟩
est une mesure de Radon sur X, car les uj sont dpsh.
   Démontrons que son support est localement contenu dans une partie squelettique. (Il
est donc contenu dans une partie squelettique de X si ce support est compact ou si X
est paracompact.)
   Plus précisément, soit U un ouvert de X, soit f : U → E une carte tropicale, soit P une
partie paralinéaire de E contenant f (U ) et soit a1 , . . . , ap des fonctions d-convexes sur P
telles que uj = f ∗ aj pour j ∈ {1, . . . , p}, soit enfin ω une forme de type (n − p, n − p)
sur P telle que α = f ∗ ω. Démontrons que la trace sur U du support de cette mesure de
                            (n)
Radon est contenu dans Σf .
   Lorsque les aj sont lisses et convexes, la restriction à U de cette mesure est égale à la
mesure associée à la forme lisse positive f ∗ (d′ d′′ a1 ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ ap ∧ ω) et son support est
                (n)
contenu dans Σf . Cette propriété est locale sur X, et est préservée par limite uniforme,
d’après la continuité du produit de courants à la Bedford–Taylor. Il en résulte qu’elle
vaut dès que les ap sont convexes. Par multilinéarité, le résultat vaut également lorsque
les aj sont d-convexes.
Remarque 8.8.7. — Lorsque les fonctions uj comme ci-dessus sont par ailleurs G-
C 2 , l’expression u0 d′ d′′ u1 ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ up peut être interprétée comme une G-forme de
type (p, p). Le lecteur prendra garde que le courant défini au §8.8.4 n’est pas en général
celui associé à cette G-forme. Ce dernier courant ne jouera d’ailleurs pas de rôle dans la
théorie, ce qui rend inoffensif cet abus de notation.
Définition 8.8.8. — Soit X un espace tropical de niveau n, sans bord. Si u est une
fonction dpsh sur X, le courant (d′ d′′ u)n est une mesure sur X, appelée mesure de
Monge-Ampère de u, et notée MA(u). Si u est psh, cette mesure est positive.
Remarque 8.8.9. — Soit f : Y → X un morphisme d’espaces tropicaux. Soit p un
entier naturel tel que p > dim(X) et soit u1 , . . . , up des fonctions dpsh sur X. Les
fonctions f ∗ u1 , . . . , f ∗ up sont dpsh et l’on a
                                 d′ d′′ (f ∗ u1 ) ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ (f ∗ up ) = 0.
   L’assertion est évidente si les ui sont lisses. Dans ce cas, le courant d′ d′′ (f ∗ u1 ) ∧ · · · ∧
d′ d′′ (f ∗ up ) = 0 est associé à la forme lisse de même nom, laquelle est l’image réciproque
de la forme d′ d′′ (u1 ) ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ (up ) sur X, et celle-ci est nulle puisque p > dim(X).
   Pour établir le cas général, on peut supposer que les ui sont psh. Les suites (u1 , . . . , up )
de fonctions psh qui vérifient la propriété requise forment un sous-faisceau de (CPshX )p .
Par construction du produit d′ d′′ (u1 ) ∧ · · · ∧ d′ d′′ (up ), il est stable par limite uniforme.
                § 8.9. UN EXEMPLE DE CALCUL DE MESURE DE MONGE-AMPÈRE                              213

Il contient les suites de fonctions lisses d’après ce qui précède, donc contient toutes les
suites de fonctions psh.

BEGIN MATHEMATICAL DATA: Unreviewed proof appendix P16.2-finite-fixed-bundle-approximants-before-original-descent-20261005-continuation113.md
# Finite fixed-bundle approximants before descending their common poles

Root continuation113, 5 October2026. This is an unreviewed repair of the
single-stage approximation detail in proposal109. It proves that detail
on the retained ample projective Y before adding sigma_D and descending.
It does not prove the converse cone identity or close original P16.2.
Active native109 and review108 have not received this note as feedback.

Retain k,X,Y,pi,L,A,D from109, with A an ACTUAL ample rational line bundle
on projective integral Y and pi^*L=A+D. The original carrier X is allowed
to be nonprojective. The retained field is algebraically closed, trivially
valued, characteristic zero, with no cardinality restriction.

## 1. A finite fixed-A family

Let F_A be the finite real-valued functions on Y^an obtained as actual
Fubini--Study section maxima for A:

    m^(-1) max_i(log ||s_i||_triv + lambda_i),

where mA is an actual integral line bundle and the finite displayed
section family s_i globally generates mA. Constants lambda_i are real.
These functions are continuous real-valued. They are genuine A-psh,
and the family is stable under adding constants and finite maxima.
For different m's, clear to a common multiple and raise the respective
sections to powers before taking their union. This preserves each
function and global generation.

The function0 belongs to F_A. Choose a globally generating family of
sections of some mA. Each has norm at most1 everywhere in the canonical
trivial metric: at the valuative center it is a regular coefficient of
a local frame of norm1. At every analytic point at least one generator
is a unit at that center, hence has norm1. The maximum logarithmic norm
is therefore exactly0. The valuative-center description is BJ1.24 on
the retained proper projective Y; it is not asserted on a hypothetical
ample bundle on nonprojective X.

For any actual nonzero s in H^0(Y,mA), put a=m^(-1)log ||s||_triv.
Clearing to a sufficiently divisible globally generated power of A,
the function max(a,-j) is in F_A: combine a raised copy of s with a
globally generating family of weight corresponding to the constant-j.
It decreases pointwise to a, including every zero of s. Thus every
single section logarithm used in BJ4.18 is a decreasing limit of
FINITE fixed-A approximants.

## 2. The finite family's single-stage decreasing closure is stable

Let D_A consist of generically finite pointwise limits of decreasing nets
from F_A. Addition of constants and finite maxima preserve D_A; for
maxima take the product of the two directed indices. We prove directly
that decreasing limits of elements of D_A stay in D_A. This uses the
compact flattening argument behind BJ4.6 only with finite continuous
inner functions.

Suppose psi_i decreases to a generically finite psi, and each psi_i is
the decreasing limit of f_{i,j} in F_A. Use indices alpha=(i,j,epsilon),
epsilon>0, and order them by

    alpha>=alpha' iff i>=i' and
                     f_{i,j}+epsilon <= f_{i',j'}+epsilon' everywhere.

This is directed. For two indices, choose i3 above both outer indices
and epsilon3 smaller than both positive epsilons. We have

    psi_i3+epsilon3 < min(f_i1,j1+epsilon1, f_i2,j2+epsilon2)

at EVERY point. The upper functions are finite, so this remains a
strict inequality where psi_i3 is minus infinity. The continuous
real-valued functions

    exp(f_i3,j+epsilon3)
       -exp(min(f_i1,j1+epsilon1, f_i2,j2+epsilon2))

decrease to a strictly negative function pointwise. Their negative open
sets increase to a cover of compact Y^an. A finite subcover and
directedness of j give one j3 for which the displayed inequality holds
globally. This provides an index above both original indices.

Now g_alpha=f_{i,j}+epsilon lies in F_A and is a decreasing net, bounded
below pointwise by psi. At any point with finite psi value, first choose
an outer i0 whose psi_i0 value is close enough to psi, then an inner j0
and epsilon0 whose g_alpha0 value is arbitrarily close from above.
Every subsequent index remains below it. At a point with psi=-infinity,
use an arbitrary real threshold instead. Thus g_alpha decreases to psi
at every point, proving stability of D_A under decreasing limits.

The restriction to finite inner approximants is useful: if both upper
approximants have a common minus-infinity value, the attempted strict
inequality -infinity+epsilon < -infinity+epsilon' is false. We do not
use that step on original L-section maxima with their common D poles.
This argument is a direct restricted proof and makes no claim that all
extended-valued families satisfy a flattening argument without further
care at their common poles.

BJ4.18 identifies PSH(Y,A) with the smallest non-identically-minus-infinity
class containing the actual A-section logarithms and stable under
constants, finite maxima and decreasing limits. Sections1--2 therefore
give

    D_A=PSH(Y,A).

The reverse inclusion also uses the genuine psh stability of decreasing
generically finite limits. No countable extraction was made; this is a
single net on the actual fixed rational line bundle A.

## 3. Literal original sections after clearing denominators

Fix psi in PSH(Y,A) and choose the finite fixed-A net psi_i above.
For every stage clear all line bundle denominators and its displayed
section degree to a common positive integer p. Raise its A-sections to
the appropriate powers and multiply each by the canonical section of
the actual integral effective divisor pD. The resulting sections belong
to p pi^*L. The raw logarithmic maximum is exactly sigma_D+psi_i.

Since X is smooth and hence normal, Stacks0AY8 and the projection formula
give

    H^0(Y,p pi^*L)=H^0(X,pL).

Every resulting section is thus the pullback of an actual original
L-section. In the canonically descended continuous L metric of109, its
logarithmic norm pulls back identically. Therefore sigma_D+psi_i is the
pullback of an actual original section maximum u_i, with exactly the
displayed constants. No numerically perturbed A_i is being descended.

The entire bad fiber is contained in D by the separate geometric proof109.
Consequently sigma_D+psi_i is minus infinity on every bad fiber and has
a unique original value on each remaining singleton fiber. This also
shows directly that it has the required original interpretation at all
reference poles. Proper analytic surjectivity then gives

    u_i down u,       pi^*u=sigma_D+psi,

pointwise on the ACTUAL original X^an. The flattening was done before
adding sigma_D, so it never needs a strictly negative exponential
difference at the original common reference poles.

In particular tau, obtained from psi=0, is itself an actual original
section maximum after clearing the fixed data. Every descended genuine
ample quotient belongs to the original FS cone and is dominated by
tau+sup psi. This establishes the forward inclusion of the full
reference-dominated cone identity. The converse division/Siu argument
is a separate dependency, not a conclusion of this approximation proof.

## 4. Exact inputs and acceptance scope

The mathematical external inputs are BJ1801.08229v4 1.24,4.18 and genuine
psh stability on projective Y, plus finite global generation of ample
powers and the actual proper birational section identity. Their pertinent
literal units were read and bound in109. The compact flattening proof
here is supplied in full; it uses only finite continuous inner maxima,
compactness, and directed nets. Canonical original metric descent and
whole bad-fiber vanishing remain explicit dependencies from109.

No independent review has assessed this new detail yet. It does not
assert that a boundedness truncation on an arbitrary nonample original
class is a finite global FS metric, nor alter the original hypotheses.

END MATHEMATICAL DATA


BEGIN MATHEMATICAL DATA: Unreviewed proof appendix P16.5-direct-nonample-FS-foundation-repair-20261005-continuation114.md
# Direct nonample FS foundations without extended-valued flattening

Root continuation114, 5 October2026. This is a new unreviewed response
to actual original review108, whose verdict is FAIL. It proposes direct
replacement proofs for the nonample homogeneous approximation,
homogeneous decomposition and zero-class cone used by draft105. It
does not override that verdict or close either original P16.5 or P16.2.

Retain a smooth integral projective X over an algebraically closed,
trivially valued characteristic-zero field of arbitrary cardinality.
Only the exact actual-ample theorem accepted99 is a task grant. For any
numerical class theta use the original BJ definition: genuine functions
are generically finite decreasing-net limits of actual generically finite
real-coefficient FS section maxima phi_i in rational line bundles L_i,
with c1(L_i) tending to theta. This defining single net is an input to
the definition, not a consequence of the false general flattening lemma.
No varying-class decreasing closure theorem is used below.

## 1. Rational coefficients directly within the defining net

Let phi_i=m_i^(-1)max_j(log||s_ij||+lambda_ij), where lambda_ij are real.
For every delta>0 round each of this finite family's coefficients upward
to a rational coefficient with error at most m_i delta. Denote the
result by f_i,delta. It is an actual rational-coefficient FS function
in the SAME actual L_i and satisfies, at every point,

    phi_i <= f_i,delta <= phi_i+delta.

At a common zero both sides are minus infinity and these non-strict
inequalities remain valid. Now put g_i,delta=f_i,delta+delta, taking
positive rational delta so g_i,delta still has rational coefficients.
Order pairs by i>=i' and g_i,delta<=g_i',delta' everywhere. This is
directed: choose i3 above two given i's and rational delta3 with
2delta3<=min(delta1,delta2). Then

    g_i3,delta3 <= phi_i3+2delta3
               <= min(phi_i1,phi_i2)+min(delta1,delta2)
               <= min(g_i1,delta1,g_i2,delta2).

All inequalities also hold at common poles. The resulting g-net decreases
pointwise to phi: first choose i approximating its desired point value,
then delta small; at a minus-infinity value use an arbitrary finite
threshold. Its line-bundle classes still tend to theta because the
pair order refines the original i order. This gives genuine rational
FS approximation directly. No compact strict-shift argument or nested
decreasing closure is needed.

## 2. Every such numerical function lies in a fixed actual ample cone

Choose an actual rational ample H with c1(H)-theta ample. Eventually
the ACTUAL rational bundle H-L_i is ample. A generating family of a
sufficiently divisible power of H-L_i has logarithmic section maximum0
in the canonical trivial metric. Multiply the sections representing
phi_i by these generators after clearing degrees. The resulting actual
H-section maximum is identically phi_i. Hence the defining decreasing
tail is a fixed actual-H net, and phi belongs to the genuine ample
PSH(X,H) cone.

Consequently the ample grant supplies finite divisorial values, generic
maximum, all-point divisorial determination, and the implication from
divisorial inequalities to any usc obstacle for every numerical phi in
this definition. This application does not need a nonample
regularization or closure theorem. Different theta's may require
different H's. Finite groups of functions can be placed in one H by
taking it sufficiently positive.

## 3. Direct homogeneous approximation

For a function phi with nonnegative generic maximum define

    hat(phi)(v)=inf_{t>0} phi(t v)/t,

where scaling t v scales the valuation and keeps its support. This
operator preserves pointwise order. If phi_i decreases to phi, then

    inf_i hat(phi_i)(v)
      =inf_i inf_t phi_i(t v)/t
      =inf_t inf_i phi_i(t v)/t
      =hat(phi)(v).

The two infima commute for arbitrary directed nets and extended values.
Each approximant can be chosen rational by Section1, still with
nonnegative generic maximum. For

    phi=m^(-1)max_j(log||s_j||+lambda_j), lambda_j rational,

hat(phi) is an ACTUAL homogeneous FS section maximum in the same
rational bundle. Here is the finite-dimensional proof. For finite real
x_j and max lambda_j>=0,

    inf_{r>=0} max_j(x_j+r lambda_j)
      =max_{w in W} sum_j w_j x_j,

where W is the finite set of vertices of

    {w_j>=0, sum w_j=1, sum w_j lambda_j>=0}.

One can verify the equality without a general duality black box. Each
weighted expression is at most the displayed max for every r. A
minimum of that convex piecewise-linear function occurs at r=0, at a
breakpoint with active slopes of opposite signs, or is its limit as
r tends to infinity. At r=0 choose an active nonnegative slope. At an
interior minimum take weights on two active slopes with weighted slope0.
In the final case max lambda=0 and choose an active zero slope. These
are vertices of the displayed polytope and give equality. Its vertices
are unit vectors of nonnegative slope, and the two-slope zero averages;
all weights are rational for rational lambda.

Clear a common denominator b of these finitely many w's. The monomials
product_j s_j^(b w_j) are sections of mbL. Their logarithmic maximum
divided by mb is exactly hat(phi). Coefficients zero in a monomial
mean that its corresponding section is absent from the product. The
same formula holds if some x_j=-infinity: replace them by max(x_j,-N),
commute the two infima over N and r, and pass through the fixed finite
maximum of products. Thus it includes all base-locus points.

The generic value of hat(phi_i) is0. The homogeneous functions decrease
to hat(phi); their actual bundle classes converge to theta. Therefore
hat(phi) is genuine by the original definition. If h is already
homogeneous, hat(h)=h. This proves exactly the nonample homogeneous
approximation required in draft105, without BJ4.15 or BJ4.6.

## 4. Direct decomposition by homogeneous components

For an arbitrary genuine phi with C=phi(v_triv), put

    h_lambda=hat(phi-lambda), lambda<C.

Section3 makes h_lambda a genuine homogeneous theta-psh function with
generic value0. Taking t=1 gives h_lambda+lambda<=phi. At every actual
point v we claim

    phi(v)=sup_{lambda<C}(h_lambda(v)+lambda).

The proof is one-dimensional. Along its scaling ray put g(t)=phi(t v).
Each defining FS approximant is convex and decreasing in t>0 and is
bounded above by its generic value. Its decreasing limit g is either
finite convex on (0,infinity), or identically minus infinity there.
Indeed a minus-infinity value forces minus infinity at all smaller t
by convexity with a finite upper-bound point, and then at all larger t
by monotonicity. The identically-minus-infinity case gives the asserted
formula immediately.

In the finite case, choose a supporting line a t+lambda0 at t=1.
The slope is nonpositive and its intercept lambda0 is at most C:
evaluate at t approaching0 and use g(t)<=C. If lambda0<C, then
h_lambda0(v)>=a and the formula follows from equality at t=1. If
lambda0=C, lower its intercept by epsilon>0. Then
h_(C-epsilon)(v)>=a and the resulting expression is at least
g(1)-epsilon. Let epsilon decrease to0. Together with the opposite
inequality h_lambda+lambda<=phi, this proves the formula at every
point, including singular-support points.

Only elementary convexity and the definition enter this proof. It
does not import the unproved varying-class closure behind BJ6.24.

## 5. The actual fixed-divisor cone follows from ample compactness

Let L=A+D with actual A ample rational and D effective rational Cartier,
and write sigma=log||s_D||. For homogeneous h<=sigma, Section3 gives
decreasing actual homogeneous FS h_i in L_i with c1(L_i)->c1(L).
On compact K={sigma=-1}, these continuous extended functions decrease
to values at most-1. For each rational 0<t<1 compactness gives eventual
h_i<-t on K. Use the cofinal directed pairs (i,t), t tending to1.
Homogeneity implies h_i<=t sigma at all divisorial valuations: if
sigma(v)<0, scale v to K; if sigma(v)=0, use h_i(v)<=0. Section2 and
the ample usc-obstacle principle extend this inequality everywhere.

Literal section division now needs no closure argument. At each
prime divisor E of smooth X the inequality says that every displayed
section has vanishing order at least t times its degree times the
coefficient of E in D. Clear denominators. Normality makes the quotient
by s_(p tD) a global section of p(L_i-tD). Hence

    r_i=h_i-t sigma

is the corresponding genuine actual FS quotient, with generic value0.
Refining the index by an additional positive rational parameter if needed,
choose rational c_i>1 tending to1 such that the ACTUAL bundle
c_i A-(L_i-tD) is ample; openness of the ample cone permits this
because L_i-tD tends numerically to A. Multiplication by a generating
family of a sufficiently divisible power of that ample difference,
whose norm maximum is0, realizes r_i/c_i as an actual A-section maximum.
These quotients need not decrease.

Normalized ample compactness supplies a convergent subnet with genuine
r in PSH(X,A). At every divisorial point its value is h-sigma, since
the explicit divided values converge there. The function sigma+r is
genuine L-psh: the finite fixed-A net of113, or exact ample fixed-bundle
regularization, multiplies by s_D to give a decreasing actual L-section
net. By Section2 both it and h lie in a common ample cone. Ample
divisorial determination gives h=sigma+r at every point.

For general u<=sigma+C0, apply Section4. For its homogeneous component
h_lambda+lambda<=u, scaling the inequality gives h_lambda<=sigma on
divisorial points by letting the scale tend to infinity. Section2's
usc-obstacle principle extends it everywhere. Divide to obtain r_lambda.
Their generic values are0, so r_lambda+lambda are uniformly bounded
above by C=u(v_triv). The exact ample envelope property yields the
genuine A-psh usc supremum r, with unchanged divisorial values. On
divisorial points u=sigma+r. The common ample embedding and divisorial
determination extend equality everywhere. Thus the ENTIRE genuine
reference-dominated cone is sigma+PSH(X,A), with unique genuine quotient.

For D=0 Section2 does not need K; the fixed actual ample datum supplies
the cone directly. No statement here makes the divided approximants
monotone or the arbitrary extended-valued flattening lemma true.

## 6. A direct constants-only zero-class cone

Let phi be genuine numerical0-psh and let its defining real FS net
phi_i in L_i have c1(L_i)->0. Put C_i=phi_i(v_triv), decreasing to
C=phi(v_triv). Refine the defining index by positive rational epsilon
decreasing to0: for every epsilon require that epsilon A-L_i is ample,
which holds eventually in i. The admissible pairs are directed by
increasing i and decreasing epsilon; for a smaller epsilon first increase
i far enough for its ample-difference condition. Set c_i,epsilon=epsilon
on this refined net, suppressing the second index below. Thus c_i>0
tends to0 and c_i A-L_i is ample for one fixed actual ample A, even if
the original defining net was stationary. The same generator multiplication as
above makes (phi_i-C_i)/c_i an actual normalized A-section maximum.

For each divisorial v, normalized ample compactness and continuous
evaluation give a finite lower bound M_v for all normalized A-psh
functions. Therefore

    c_i M_v <= phi_i(v)-C_i <=0.

Passing to the limit gives phi(v)=C at every divisorial point.
Section2 places phi in a fixed actual ample H cone; the constant C
also belongs to that cone. Ample divisorial determination implies
phi=C on all of X^an. This proves PSH(0)=R without applying a
varying-class decreasing closure theorem to the sequence m phi.
It supplies the missing foundation for the actual trivial-bundle
zero-mass calculations of draft105.

## 7. Review obligations

This is a proposed proof repair, requiring fresh independent assessment.
The precise mathematical inputs are the original FS defining net,
canonical trivial metric and literal section arithmetic, global
generation of ample powers, openness of the ample cone, normal
divisibility, and the exact genuine-AMPLE compactness, envelope and
divisorial principles already granted99. The general Lemma4.6 is
neither assumed nor repaired. Its actual108 counterexample is preserved.
No original acceptance is asserted by this root note. The remaining
genuine operator proofs and source applications still require their
own full reconstruction.

END MATHEMATICAL DATA


BEGIN MATHEMATICAL DATA: Unreviewed proof appendix P16.2-compatible-valuative-centers-and-local-factoriality-20261005-continuation115.md
# Proper original centers and the actual canonical metric

Root continuation115, 5 October2026. This is an unreviewed source-and-proof
continuation for original P16.2. Actual author109 remains incomplete;
actual independent P16.5 review108 remains FAIL. No original is closed.
Native authors112/113 receive their frozen packets; this note is not hot
feedback and does not inspect their private work.

## 1. The precise valuative premise

Use Stacks tag0BX5, current Lemma29.43.1: a finite-type quasi-separated
scheme morphism is proper exactly when every valuation-ring diagram
has a unique extension. The printed proof is a formal consequence of
the scheme universal-closedness and separatedness criteria. It is not
a separately printed long proof. The scheme version suffices here;
no algebraic-space extension of the fraction field is involved.

The actual primary bodies additionally opened and read were:

* Section26.20, tag01KA, including Lemma26.20.5 and Proposition26.20.6.
  The former's printed proof constructs the map from the closed-point
  local ring to the given valuation ring by maximality under domination.
* Lemma26.22.1, tag01KZ, with its entire printed proof: the equalizer of
  two extensions is closed and contains the generic point of the
  valuation-ring spectrum, so its defining ideal is zero in a domain.

These are the pertinent existence and uniqueness mechanisms. Earlier
topological and algebraic cross-references were not all read recursively.
The current page numbering is recorded as retrieved; old book PDFs may
have different lemma numbers. The permanent tags fix the statements.

## 2. Center of every analytic point on an arbitrary proper original X

Let X be a proper finite-type k-scheme, with k trivially valued, and
x in X^an. Its support and completed residue field define an actual
scheme morphism Spec H(x) -> X over k. Put R_x=H(x)^circ, the subring
of elements of absolute value at most1. For any nonzero element a,
either a or a^(-1) lies in R_x; hence R_x is a valuation ring with
fraction field H(x). The trivial norm on k makes k a subring of R_x.

Properness supplies a unique map Spec R_x -> X extending the given
generic map. Its closed-point image is the center c_X(x). This applies
to every analytic point, including points with nongeneric support, and
to every cardinality of k. No projectivity or rank-one discreteness
condition enters the valuation-ring criterion.

Let f:Y->X be a k-morphism between proper finite-type schemes and
y in Y^an, with x=f^an(y). The analytic functor gives an isometric
embedding H(x)->H(y). Thus R_x maps locally to R_y: the intersection
with R_x of the maximal ideal {a:|a|<1} of R_y is the maximal ideal
of R_x. Compose Spec R_y -> Y with f. It extends the same generic
map as Spec R_y -> Spec R_x -> X, so uniqueness on X gives equality.
In particular

    f(c_Y(y))=c_X(f^an(y)).

The identical proof works for any isometric valued-field extension
and an analytic point above x. It uses the actual residue-field map,
not a choice of a lift. It proves the compatible-center assertion
needed in original canonical metric pullback and section descent.

## 3. Literal canonical metric functoriality

For an actual line bundle L on proper X, choose a local algebraic frame
e at c_X(x). The canonical trivial norm assigns norm1 to that frame at
x; a vector a e has norm |a|. This does not depend on the frame: their
ratio is a unit of O_(X,c_X(x)), whose image and inverse both lie in
R_x, so its absolute value is1. A local regular coefficient has norm
at most1 for the same local-ring-map reason.

The compatible-center equality makes f^*e a frame at c_Y(y). It follows
directly that the canonical metric on f^*L is the pullback of this
canonical metric on L. Tensor products and divisible rational powers
are compatible by multiplying or taking the appropriate power of the
coefficient norms. For an actual section s of a cleared power, its
logarithmic norm therefore satisfies

    log||f^*s||(y)=log||s||(f^an(y)),

including the value minus infinity at a zero. This establishes the
actual metric/section pullback used in109/113 on nonprojective X.
It does not assert that the canonical metric of an arbitrary line
bundle is globally continuous or semipositive. Continuity of the
specified retained reference follows instead from its actual finite
FS representation and the compact quotient argument of110.

For a globally generating family of a cleared actual line bundle,
all its normalized section norms are at most1 at each center. Some
member is a unit coefficient in the local frame: otherwise the
family would not generate its stalk. Its norm is1. Hence the maximum
of logarithmic norms is identically0, also on a proper nonprojective
carrier. This is the generator multiplication identity used in114.

## 4. Local factoriality source audit

The actual full printed statement and proof of Stacks tag0AG0,
current Lemma15.123.2, were opened and read. A regular local ring is
a UFD. The proof inducts on dimension, chooses x outside m^2, makes
x prime using the regular quotient, and principalizes each height-one
prime after localization using Pic(R_x)=0. The final irreducible
factor remains prime before localization because x is prime.

The immediately preceding tag0AFZ, Lemma15.123.1, and its entire
printed proof were also opened and read. It proves Pic(R_f)=0 using
a finite free resolution and its determinant. Its earlier finite-free
resolution, determinant and elementary factorization cross-references
are published algebraic background; their complete bodies were not
all read in this continuation. This source audit therefore supplies
the pertinent proof units without claiming a recursive new proof of
all commutative algebra.

On the smooth varieties in the retained domain, local rings are
regular. The perfect-field regular/smooth equivalence was separately
bound to Stacks0B8X in112. Thus local factoriality is applied precisely
where needed for Cartier boundary/prime-divisor statements. Normal
section division is a separate assertion and must still use its own
literal normality/division mechanism; the UFD citation does not replace
that proof or the genuine fixed-divisor cone construction.

## 5. Evidence and remaining scope

The native web responses are retained locally under
P16.2-continuation115-valuative-primary. Exact selected source bodies,
URLs and renderer coordinates will be bound without manually changing
their literal wording. Whole primary works and all recursively cited

The intrinsic original local unbounded operator remains a separate

BEGIN MATHEMATICAL DATA: Unreviewed proof appendix P16.2-common-ample-collar-and-intrinsic-generated-local-operator-20261005-continuation117.md
# A common actual ample collar for original local section weights

Root continuation117, 5 October2026. This is a new unreviewed reconstruction
of the common-collar obligation isolated in116. Neither P16.2 nor P16.5
is marked solved by it. Native authors112/113 work on their own unchanged
packets; their private outputs have not been read or used.

Retain the datum and genuine cone hypotheses of116. The field is any
algebraically closed trivially valued characteristic-zero k. In this note
all local approximants are actual finite rational affine FS weights on
one ordinary neighborhood, with bounded decreasing endpoints. Real
constants can be handled by the direct upward-rounding net explained
below. No arbitrary classical CLD psh/current density theorem is assumed.
The complete associated original cone is not reduced: its bounded
reference floors and actual local section approximants have exactly
these weights, and all E1 endpoints are recovered from their floors.

## 1. The only local bridge, applied on an actual ample projective carrier

The exact G0 mathematical body, Section2, states the compact contact
theorem and its continuous contact metric bridge in its projective,
actual-ample domain. The continuous bridge concerns an ALREADY GLUED
continuous rational metric on an actual ample rational H, whose local
weights have finite unit-log affine supports below the weight, equal
at the point with the SAME additive constant. It makes that metric
genuinely H-psh. This is the precise input used below, ONLY on the
smooth projective Y and an actual ample H. It is not applied to the
original nonprojective X or its nonample L.

The complete earlier reconstruction of that bridge is saved as
public-P16.1-round-92-final-reader/granted/supplied-02.txt, Sections2-3.
Its historical labels are documentary, not new grants. The pertinent
body was read at LF25-110. It first constructs exact original algebraic
PL extensions using clipped section logarithms, actual principal
localization and finite rational polyhedral subdivision. Flag ideals
give an algebraic test configuration on the SAME actual H. Over k((t))
the pulled unit-log supports give the curve maximum test. The GR and GM
model criteria test whole proper residual curves and imply nefness of
that supplied algebraic model. BE7.8 is used only over this NONTRIVIAL
extension. Gauss evaluation of its pure FS sections gives an EXACT
finite coefficient cutoff in original H sections: all coefficient
norms are at most1 and the continuous metric has a compact lower bound.
Uniform FS approximants with summable shifts decrease in that fixed H.

This lists the actual route and its primary background; it is not a
new proof of every recursively cited theorem. Those primary bodies are
in the supplied G0 source dictionary. Every new application must check
their exact hypotheses. No general varying-class/pole flattening lemma,
arbitrary formal-line-bundle algebraization, Laurent-cover positivity
criterion, or unrestricted current converse is used by this bridge.

## 2. Exact common geometric neighborhood and reference

Fix a point of V=X^an\P and identify its neighborhood with an ordinary
open of Y^an\D. Shrink to a principal algebraic chart where the original
L frame, the A frame and D section factor are defined and the D factor
is a unit. Basic seminorm neighborhoods permit finitely many strict
rational upper/lower bounds on regular functions; required nonvanishing
functions are inverted in this same chart. Append a complete very ample
section system so that its ratios include a generating set of the
original affine chart ring. These generator bounds make the closed
seminorm domain compact. They do not require strict affinoidness over
the trivial field or a countable field.

The explicit construction at supplied-02.txt LF115-117 clears the
principal denominators and builds ONE actual FS local reference weight
v on an actual rational ample H, with rational epsilon>0 and

    Q={v<=0} compactly contained in the given ordinary neighborhood,
    P0={v<=-epsilon}, and the point in interior(P0).

The local raw expression, with tensor denominator q, is

    v=(1/q) max(q lambda, log|F_j/a^m|-C_j).

The thresholds are C_j, without an erroneous extra factor q. A negative
constant branch and the appended section system give a finite weight.
Clearing its denominator produces actual H sections. Write v=h0+p,
where h0 is the canonical H raw weight in that frame and p is the
corresponding GLOBAL actual finite FS potential. It is the same p and
the same H for every approximation to be compared. Local h0 is finite
continuous on Q, and all cocycles are those of that actual H.

For compact tests it is enough to make this construction at each point
of their support and take a finite cover. There is no claim that one
principal chart contains an arbitrary large compact set.

## 3. One fixed collar for finitely many nets, including independent nets

Let w_i be a finite tuple of bounded raw local weights on a common
ordinary neighborhood of Q. Each is the pointwise decreasing limit
of finite rational AFS/contact weights w_(i,alpha). Include BOTH nets
for any one endpoint to be compared, as well as a bounded test weight
if needed. Choose rational M>0 so -M<=w_i<=M on Q. Choose rational R>0
so R epsilon>2M+2, and set

    h=R v+M+1.

Then h<-M on P0. On the compact outer strip
S=Q intersect {v>=-1/(2R)}, h>=M+1/2. For each net the compact closed
sets {w_(i,alpha)>=M+1/4} in S decrease to the empty set. Compactness
and directedness give an eventual tail empty on the WHOLE strip.
Choose such tails for the finite tuple independently. This is a
finite-intersection argument for arbitrary nets, not uniform convergence
to a discontinuous endpoint.

On these tails define functions on the ENTIRE Y^an:

    Psi_(i,alpha)=max(w_(i,alpha)-h,0) on Q, and0 outside Q,
    Phi_(i,alpha)=p+Psi_(i,alpha)/R.

The strict margin on S makes Psi identically0 on one whole fixed outer
collar. Hence these are globally continuous actual H metrics: their
raw weights in the inner chart are

    (1/R) max(w_(i,alpha)-M-1, R v),

and on the collar and outside they are the raw weights of p. The
original H transition functions are unchanged. The finite AFS weights
have their unit-log branch supports; an inactive zero branch is deleted
on a sufficiently small neighborhood. The max with v preserves those
same-constant supports. These verify exactly the continuous contact
bridge of Section1. Thus Phi_(i,alpha) is genuinely H-psh in one FIXED
actual ample bundle, including all field cardinalities.

They decrease pointwise, since only the w terms vary. Their explicit
global limit is

    Phi_i=p+(1/R) max(w_i-h,0) on Q, and p outside Q.

It is bounded and hence genuine finite energy by fixed-ample decreasing
closure. At every point in interior(P0), the floor is inactive even
at each finite stage: w_(i,alpha)>=w_i>=-M>h. Consequently

    w_(i,alpha)=R Phi_(i,alpha)+R h0+M+1,
    w_i=R Phi_i+R h0+M+1.

The exact curvature identity is

    dd^c w_i = R(c1(H)+dd^c Phi_i) in this local construction.

The canonical reference term R h0 cannot be dropped. Psi/R by itself
is not the actual H-potential. The local mixed measure is R^n times
the restriction of the genuine RAW H mixed measure of the Phi_i.

## 4. Independence and weighted bounded local convergence

At finite continuous stages the displayed raw weights are identical
to the given original local weights on interior(P0). The BE/CLD
continuous product identification therefore makes their genuine H
products, with factors R, the actual local products of those weights.
Compact continuous tests extend by zero to Y. G0 bounded decreasing-net
continuity gives the local limits.

If two independently supplied local nets have endpoint w_i on Q,
the construction uses the SAME h,p,H and R. Their global limits Phi_i
are equal at EVERY point, by the explicit formula, including outside
Q. G0 therefore gives exactly the same local limiting measure. This
proves independence of the locally supplied nets in this generated
category. Different initial collars are compared on a third shared
smaller collar: every continuous finite-stage product agrees on its
inner open, and compact tests pass those identities to each genuine
bounded limit. The positive local Radon measures consequently glue
and their restrictions agree.

For a bounded local test w0, the same collar yields
w0=R Phi0+R h0+M+1 near the tested support. The Phi0 term has genuine
weighted continuity by G0, with the test-containing pairing MINUS the
zero-test pairing. The fixed local reference term is continuous and
passes by ordinary weak convergence against compact tests. Differences
of tests are split into their terms. Thus the bounded local weighted
products and their limits are approximation-independent as well.

Rational constants are sufficient. For an existing finite real-constant
AFS net, round its finitely many constants upward with uniform error
delta and add delta. The pair index (alpha,delta) is ordered by increasing
alpha and pointwise decreasing resulting weights. Choosing alpha above
two old indices and 2delta below both old deltas gives directedness
by the non-strict inequalities already proved in114. The resulting
net decreases to the SAME endpoint on the same ordinary neighborhood.
Its finite-stage uniform error tends to0, and the actual continuous
product/finite reference construction is compatible with that limit.
More explicitly, for each real-constant stage its rational roundings,
with error sufficiently small to preserve the fixed outer strip margin,
give rational-contact GLOBAL metrics in the same H. The max operation
is1-Lipschitz, so they approach that stage's explicit Phi uniformly on
all Y, with error at most2delta/R. Fixed-ample uniform closure and
continuous product compatibility make this real-constant Phi genuine
as well. The ORIGINAL real-constant Phi net therefore decreases to the
same explicit global endpoint, and G0 applies directly to it. No
interchange of unrelated local and global approximating limits is needed.
No claim about arbitrary extended-valued varying families is made.

## 5. Local maxima and reference-floor nonpluripolar compatibility

One collar for a finite tuple commutes with taking its maxima: the
explicit global potential for max(w,z) is max(Phi_w,Phi_z), since
p,h and R are identical. G0 genuine strict-winning locality and its
mixed version therefore give local strict-winning locality for bounded
generated endpoints, on the actual Borel winning sets. No continuity
of the endpoints is needed.

For the original E1 endpoint w, the retained reference b is the local
raw weight of tau. It is finite continuous on compact frame opens
inside V. The reference floors max(w,b-j) are bounded there and belong
to the generated category: apply max to actual decreasing original
section approximants and the finite original reference weight. Their
intrinsic local products have just been constructed independently of
local approximants. Sections1-4 of116 identify these products with the
restrictions of the actual ample quotient floor products.

For completeness, reference independence in this LOCAL generated
construction can be checked without inferring equality merely from
equal endpoint values on a Borel set. Let b' be another finite continuous
generated local reference and bound |b-b'| by C on Q. For fixed j,k,
choose ell larger than both by more than C and use the bounded
generated lower floor

    z_ell=max(w,b-ell,b'-ell).

On a smaller neighborhood of the tested compact set,

    max(w,b-j)=max(z_ell,b-j),
    max(w,b'-k)=max(z_ell,b'-k).

The common-collar bounded strict-winning theorem compares both products
with the product of z_ell on their respective strict winning sets.
They agree on the intersection of those sets. As j and k increase,
these sets cover exactly {w>-infinity}. On each fixed winning set all
later restricted products agree; the restricted measures are therefore
increasing and have the same local limit. The complement, where w is
minus infinity, is assigned zero mass in this nonpluripolar construction.
For mixed products use a common ell and the joint winning set for the
finite tuple. This argument concerns generated continuous references;
it does not assert a density theorem for an arbitrary CLD psh reference.

Because the retained quotient is actual E1_A, G0 integrability and the
separately reconstructed E1 pole on D identify this nonpluripolar local
limit with the endpoint ample measure restricted to Y\D. Its original
zero extension has raw mass A^n and gives P zero mass. Thus the stronger
bounded-local-approximation comparison needed in109's gate L is supplied
in the precise locally generated section/contact category associated
with this datum, before any target measure.

## 6. Every requested unbounded mixed and integrable weighted limit

The global original associated cone is the ENTIRE proposed dominated
cone, by the independently required114 converse. Its E1 space consists
of actual original metrics with genuine ample E1 quotients. Section5
constructs their local operator and reference-floor nonpluripolar
extension. For any decreasing net of such metrics with E1 limits,
the all-point quotient order gives genuine decreasing quotient nets.
G0 unbounded mixed continuity tests them by every original continuous
function after pullback. This supplies the original unbounded mixed
weak limit, not only the bounded local limit of Section4.

An integrable actual original quotient test equals its unique quotient
on V. Every output gives P zero mass. G0 weighted continuity therefore
gives the original weighted measures with the correct pairing sign.
On a compact local frame open, raw test weights differ from that
quotient by a fixed finite continuous reference term. That term passes
by weak convergence, so the same local raw weighted identities hold.
Absolute global integrability of sigma is not required or asserted.
Arbitrary local unbounded functions outside this associated positivity
category and unrelated classical current multiplication are not inputs
to the proposed equation theory.

## 7. Exact conclusion and review obligations

The deduction here uses G0 ONLY on actual ample bundles on projective Y.
It applies the explicitly stated continuous contact bridge in exactly
that domain, verifies one common ordinary neighborhood, rational
same-constant contacts, actual frames/cocycles, fixed ample reference,
compact strip tail, and common all-point global endpoint. The formulas
make local-net, collar, frame and generated-reference independence
explicit. Genuine bounded local products precede unbounded reference
floors, and the entire construction precedes any target.

The needed nonlocal premises are the actual retained datum, saturated
polar image, actual original section descent, whole genuine cone/order,
and actual E1 polar test. They are still unreviewed applications to
reconstruct in the full original paper, using the115 center audit and
other proposed proofs. G0's complete local bridge and its primary
background must also be checked for this new application. No original
PASS, completion count or source-certification override is asserted.
This is a proposed repair of the precise operator gate; the unchanged

BEGIN MATHEMATICAL DATA: Unreviewed proof appendix P16.5-effective-divisorial-zero-volume-prescribed-type-equations-20261005-continuation118.md
# Zero-mass equations in the entire effective-divisor reference cone

Root continuation118, 5 October2026. This is a new UNREVIEWED extension
of the zero-volume part of original P16.5. It does not override actual
FAIL108, declare either running native author complete, or increment
the original solution count. Only the exact genuine actual-AMPLE G0
is granted. The single-defining-net foundations in114 are proposals
whose direct proofs must be reconstructed in the full paper.

Let X be smooth integral projective of POSITIVE dimension over any
algebraically closed trivially valued characteristic-zero k. Let D
be ANY effective rational Cartier divisor and L=O_X(D) the retained
actual rational line bundle. Fix its actual divisor reference
sigma=log||s_D||. Define the whole genuine reference-dominated cone

    C_D={u in genuine PSH(c1(L)): u<=sigma+C0 for some finite C0}.

The following construction works for every effective D. Its application
to the additional original ZERO-VOLUME range restricts to vol(L)=0.
The retained reference is the full divisor, without an ample moving
part. Its associated nonpluripolar mass is0. This mass is not claimed
to equal an arbitrary self-intersection D^n or the volume of a big D.

## 1. Homogeneous division with a vanishing ample scale

We claim that any homogeneous genuine h in this D-class, with generic
value0 and h<=sigma, equals sigma at EVERY point.

Use114's direct rational-rounding/homogeneous projection argument on
ONE defining FS net. It yields actual homogeneous rational FS h_i
in rational L_i with c1(L_i)->c1(L) and h_i decreasing to h. Its proof
uses finite rational section monomials, not BJ's false general4.6
or its varying-class closure. By114's fixed ample embedding, h,h_i
and sigma have finite divisorial values and satisfy the genuine ample
usc-obstacle implication on an auxiliary actual ample H.

For D nonzero, K={sigma=-1} is compact. For every rational0<t<1,
the continuous extended h_i eventually satisfy h_i<-t on K, by a
compact open-cover argument for the decreasing net. Use the cofinal
admissible pairs (i,t), with i increasing and t tending to1.
Homogeneity gives h_i<=t sigma on all divisorial points: scale any
negative-sigma point to K, and use h_i<=0 where sigma=0. The fixed
ample usc-obstacle principle extends the inequality to all points.

Clear all rational powers. At each prime E, every section in h_i
has vanishing order at least its degree times t coeff_E(D). Literal
normal section division by s_(m tD) therefore gives an actual
homogeneous FS quotient r_i in B_i=L_i-tD. Its generic value is0,
and its classes tend to0. At each divisorial v its actual value is

    r_i(v)=h_i(v)-t sigma(v),

which is finite and tends to h(v)-sigma(v). No decreasing order of
these divided quotients is asserted.

Choose one actual rational ample A0. Refine the admissible index by
positive rational epsilon tending to0, requiring epsilon A0-B_i
ample. For each epsilon this holds on a tail because c1(B_i)->0.
The refined index is directed: for a smaller epsilon first increase
(i,t) far enough for that condition and above both previous pairs.
It is cofinal in the original admissible net. This also handles an
original stationary net; no nonexistent decreasing index function
is required.

The norm maximum of a globally generating family of a sufficiently
divisible power of epsilon A0-B_i is identically0. Multiply actual
sections and clear powers. This makes

    q_i=r_i/epsilon

an ACTUAL normalized A0-FS potential. Normalized genuine ample
compactness and continuous finite divisorial evaluation give, for
each divisorial v, one finite lower bound M_v for every such q_i.
The generic maximum0 also gives q_i(v)<=0. Thus

    epsilon M_v <= r_i(v) <=0.

Let epsilon tend to0 along the refined net. We obtain h(v)=sigma(v)
at every divisorial point. Place both genuine h and sigma in the same
actual ample H using114's direct embedding. G0 divisorial determination
then gives h=sigma at ALL points, including the divisor and other
singular-support points. For D=0 the direct constants-only zero-class
proof of114 gives the same conclusion without K or division.

## 2. The ENTIRE prescribed cone, not just displayed section metrics

For u in C_D put C=u(v_triv), a finite real number. The direct
one-dimensional convex proof in114 gives the all-point decomposition

    u=sup_(lambda<C)(h_lambda+lambda),
    h_lambda(v)=inf_(r>0)(u(r v)-lambda)/r.

Each h_lambda is genuine homogeneous in the same numerical D-class,
with generic value0. Scale h_lambda+lambda<=u<=sigma+C0 on divisorial
points and let r tend to infinity. All relevant divisorial values
are finite, so h_lambda<=sigma there. The auxiliary ample obstacle
principle makes this inequality hold everywhere. Section1 therefore
gives h_lambda=sigma for EVERY lambda<C. The decomposition implies

    u=sigma+C at every point.

Conversely sigma+C is genuine, being one actual D-section metric plus
a constant, and satisfies the prescribed bound. Consequently

    C_D={sigma+C:C in R}.

This proves the whole genuinely defined cone. It is not merely the
span of a selected reference, an abstract quotient, or a finite-section
calculation. The numerical approximation definition and all-point
closure issues are handled through the explicit single-net proofs.

## 3. Actual local metric operator before a target

On X^an\D choose an actual L frame e and write s_D=f e. The raw local
metric weight of sigma+C is log|f|+C: the canonical reference h_L
in sigma=log|f|-h_L cancels when that local metric weight is formed.
Both f and f^(-1) are analytic on this open. CLD18.1.2 makes its
Hessian0, so all positive-degree mixed local metric products are0.
Changing frames adds another unit logarithm with zero Hessian.

The reference floors are max(sigma+C,sigma-j)=sigma+max(C,-j), which
remain in the SAME genuine cone and have the same zero local products.
Their strict-winning nonpluripolar extension across D is therefore
the zero positive Radon measure on the ORIGINAL X. This is a genuine
local/reference-floor operator constructed before any target:

    MA_(D,raw)(sigma+C)=0, of raw mass0.

Mixed products of finitely many potentials in this cone are also0.
Every decreasing-net and integrable-weight limit is consequently
exactly0. No probability division or positive-intersection formula
is used. The singular reference is retained and its absolute
integrability is not part of the definition.

## 4. Energy, envelopes, all continuous directions and actual attainment

The unique genuine relative quotient is the finite constant C, obtained
from divisorial values before any subtraction at a pole. Set

    E_D(sigma+C)=0, and E1_D=C_D.

This agrees with the relative mixed-integral expression since all
positive-degree local products and reference-floor measures are0.
It has monotonicity, concavity, cocycle and decreasing continuity.
The normalized genuine cone, fixed by C=0, is one point and compact.

For b in C(X^an), the relative envelope of sigma+b is

    R_D(sigma+b)=sigma+inf_(X^an)b.

Indeed sigma+C<=sigma+b forces C<=b on every divisorial point,
where sigma is finite. The genuine ample usc-obstacle implication
applied to the constant C makes C<=b everywhere. The converse is
immediate, including at reference poles. Thus all-point reference
envelopes are actual functions, and extended continuity follows
from continuity of sigma. This proof does not rely on cancellation
of two minus-infinity values or mere dense-set usc equality.

For an actual u=sigma+C and any original f in C(X^an), the cone
below the obstacle u+t f is nonempty: sigma+C-|t|norm(f) is a
candidate. Its energy supremum is0, so its two-sided derivative
at0 is0, equal to the integral of f against the zero local operator.
This includes ALL continuous original directions. No positive-volume
coercivity estimate is needed on the normalized singleton.

The only positive Radon measure of mass0 is mu=0. Its intrinsic
relative dual supremum is

    sup_(C in R)(E_D(sigma+C)-integral C dmu)=0.

It is finite and attained by EVERY actual sigma+C. The all-E1
quotient-integrability condition also holds. Each such potential
solves its zero-mass equation, and any two actual solutions differ
by one real constant. Comparison and locality are immediate for
the zero measures. Thus all compactness, envelope, derivative,
target, actual-attainment and uniqueness requirements are explicit
in this full prescribed type, rather than probability-normalizing0.

## 5. A nontrivial zero-volume example and the dimension-zero guard

On X=P1 times P1, retain an actual fiber divisor D of a projection.
Its bundle has h0(mD)=m+1, hence volume0 in dimension2. The reference
sigma_D has that whole fiber as its retained pole. The theorem above
applies to the ENTIRE sigma_D-dominated genuine cone, which consists
of sigma_D+C, with the genuine zero local/nonpluripolar operator.
The unrestricted PSH(c1(D)) cone can have many other metrics, so this
does not claim uniqueness for that different unrestricted equation.

The additional zero-volume range in this note has dimension at least1.
In dimension0, a smooth integral proper variety over algebraically
closed k is Spec k, and genuine potentials are finite constants.
Its degree-zero wedge is its actual fundamental point measure of
mass1; the usual line-bundle volume is1. Assigning it a zero measure
would be incorrect. The direct PSH(0)=R assertion still holds, but
the ZERO-MASS theorem is not applied to that point case.

## 6. Review boundary

This adds EVERY actual effective-divisor zero-volume bundle in its
retained full-divisor reference type to the proposed zero-volume
domain. A pseudoeffective class without a retained effective divisor
representation is not asserted covered. The full big-reference
theorem and other original field/geometric axes remain separate.

Every external application must be reconstructed: canonical metric
and generating sections, actual ample openness/global generation,
normal section division, genuine ample compactness/divisorial
principles, and CLD analytic-unit Hessian. The direct114 single-net
foundations are indispensable, not a grant for a false general lemma.
No whole primary reading or formal proof certificate is claimed.
This is a proposal for a larger honest zero-volume range, requiring
fresh full-original paper integration and independent review.
